Converting an object (or a hash-map object) to such a union tries, in order: an object of a member's own copy shape (as it is); the members that declare every property of it that another member declares (`covering`), as it is, then as its properties can be read as they are (`object_readable`, `readable_as`: a declared type implying the property's in another representation, a number where `number | string` goes, a closure, which a read adapts, also an optional one, or a nested object whose properties are read so, review round 3's R3-2), then by a copy, first into the members its declared types resemble (`resembles`: the member's types up to `any`, so an erased copy's `{ value: { n: any } }` goes to `{ value: { n: number } }` before `{ value: Record<string, number> }`); then the other members, as it is, then by a copy; then iterator results (D-178). A class's instance (or an error) is copied into a member's shape only if no member takes it as it is, so it keeps its class and methods (review round 2's G2, G3). A value that no member declaring its properties takes (a lie through `any`, an erased copy's `Box<any>`) is taken as before, and typed reads then convert it or throw. A copy leaves an absent optional property out and is frozen if its source was (D-157). The members each property is declared by are computed once per union check (a table shared by threads), and an object's members once per descriptor and union (a per-thread four-way cache), so conversions cost about what they did (callgrind, `PTS_THREADS=1`, per conversion: typed objects through `any` 789 → 499 instructions, class instances 780 → 772, parsed JSON records 3,888 → 3,264; erased copies' objects of 40 unions in turn 2,407 → 2,437, of 300 unions 2,402 → 2,482, with a four-way cache). `pts_check_implies` applies the same rule to a union target (a type implies a member only if that member declares every property of it another member declares), so an object whose field holds such a union is copied rather than kept.
  - **Dynamic values go into union-typed places with a check** (`lower`: `convert`, `trustsDynamic`), as into other typed places, unless a collection of typed elements holds them: an element its own builtin method reads (`pop`, `at`, `get`, a callback's `find`), or a conditional of those, goes in unchecked as before. So a call through a union of object types' dynamic result (D-191's erased copy's: `const xs: number[] | undefined = pick(0).list(7)` read 8.5 for 8, a lie crashed), a value declared `any` or `unknown` that a type guard narrowed (parsed JSON narrowed to `number[] | string` read 7.125 for 6.5), wherever they are held (a variable, a class's or interface's field, an `unknown[]`'s element, a record's value, an `unknown[]`'s `find` with a type guard) and inside `||`, `&&`, a comma or a conditional, and an awaited, yielded or `then` value, are converted with a check. A member that dynamic data has no copy shape for (an object type known only from literals with getters) converts objects as they are only (`prepareCheckedUnion`); a union with a member no check describes keeps the unchecked conversion, as before. An object literal's object that the member it is one of as it is leaves out a property holding dynamic values inside it (an `any[]`, a `{ n: any }`) that another member declares (`{ tag: "some", value: anyArray }` where only `{ tag: "none" }` is provided, `leavesOut`) converts to the union as dynamic data does, so it is copied into that member; a property of another typed type (a `Dog[]`, a function of fewer parameters) or of `any` itself, and a class's instance, stay as they are, as before, and typed reads of them verify them.
  - **Maps and sets convert by a checked copy** (`convert_map`), as arrays do (D-071): one of the target's descriptor stays the same map; another is copied with each value converted and each key as it is (a copied key would no longer find what the program looks it up by), in its order. D-071's "maps aren't copied" made an erased copy's `Map<string, any>` a TypeError where tsc says `Map<string, number>`.
  - **H09b1's restriction narrowed.** D-191 made no demand where a parameter typed with the method's type parameters is of an object type, an interface or a union of them (`verifiesObjects`), since an instance read such objects unverified. Now only a dynamic value, or inside a union of object types an instance of a generic class, keeps the call on the erased copy (`holdsUnverified`): an erased copy's `Box<any>` never converts to `Box<number>`, so its object stays the member that leaves it out, which the instance would read as the other member and throw at, where the erased copy reads it dynamically; a dynamic value's place (`never[]`'s elements) the erased copy reads as undefined where an instance throws (C194). The skipped `run/generic_method_object_arguments.pts` runs, except an object literal written as such an argument (C188, H09b3).
- **Reason:** C187 is memory safety (tie-breaker 1): a typed read must give a value of its type or throw, never reinterpret a dynamic value's bits. Verifying at reads is the net, since which conversions fed an object isn't known statically; preferring the member that declares an object's properties, and converting dynamic values into unions with a check, puts honest programs' objects (erased copies of `Opt<T>`, `Option<T>`, parsed JSON) into the member they are read as, so they print what Node prints (tie-breaker 2): the reproducer's 567-case matrix goes from 247 OK, 165 wrong values and 8 crashes to 471 OK, 1 wrong value (a getter copied as a value, C190) and no crash; the remaining TypeErrors are lies through `any` (D-L09: a typed place checks an `any`) and erased copies' `Box<any>` (C169, H09b3).
- **Alternatives rejected:**
  - Copying at a typed read (the reproducer's direction (a)): a write into the copy is lost (`o.value.push(8)`), identity changes at every read, and a loop reading an erased array copied it each time (3,064 instructions per read against 63; this design reads it at 64).
  - Copying an object into a member that leaves out its properties when none that declares them converts (the reproducer's direction (b)): `{ tag: "some", value: [7] }` with a value that doesn't convert became `{ tag: "some" }`, so console.log and the erased copy's dynamic reads lost data the base printed (`r12`, `r14`).
  - Recognising the untrusted sources by their shape (a union call's result, a narrowed identifier: review round 1's snapshot): `||`, a comma, a field or an element of `unknown` still went in unchecked (review round 1, F1); trusting only typed collections' reads covers them all.
  - Copying every typed object that a union's member leaves a property of (review round 1's snapshot): an honest class instance became a plain object (`instanceof` false, later pushes lost, a 1,000-element array copied at each call; review round 1, F2); and at run time, copying class instances or objects whose properties sites read as they are into the covering member (review round 2's snapshot): an awaited `Product` lost its methods, an awaited literal its identity (G2).
  - Reading an array of a subtype's elements as one of its supertype's through a union member as it is (the base): a write through that view puts another subtype's object into it, which typed code then misreads.
  - Literal types in the IR's unions, which would let a conversion pick the member by its discriminant's value: interned interfaces and unions would split by tag, which every conversion, `passesAsIs` and `provides` compares; left for a later task (C189).
  - Checking every dynamic value that goes into a union-typed place: `find` over a typed array costs 411 → 636 instructions per call for values its typed source already holds.
  - Keeping D-191's restriction: the skipped test's honest calls keep throwing `Expected Box<User>, got Box<any>`.
- **Supersedes:** D-072's "Reads of a field or getter throw TypeError if it holds another kind than the site reads ... which only a compiler bug could cause" and D-073's read rule (a read verifies the declared type and converts a value of another type as it is); D-071's "a union takes the first member the value fits as it is, else the first it converts to" for unions of object types; D-071's "maps or sets of the target's descriptor" (others are copied); the lowering's unchecked `ToUnion` for dynamic values other than typed collections' elements; D-191's `verifiesObjects` restriction.
- **Known gaps:** an object literal written as the argument of an object literal's generic method for a parameter of a union of object types makes no demand (C188, H09b3: `generic_method_literal_arguments.pts`, skipped); a value that converts by a copy to several members with the same property names (an object-typed and a `Record`-typed `size`) takes the first in the IR's order, so parsed JSON may be copied into the wrong member and its narrowed read throws, as on the base (C189); an erased copy's getter is read at the union's copy, so console.log prints its value where Node prints `[Getter]` (C190, as D-071's copies do); a copy is another object than the one the erased copy or typed code holds, so a write through one doesn't show in the other (D-071; review round 1's `b06`), also for an object literal or an interface's object holding an `any[]` given to a union (copied, `leavesOut`); an erased copy's object with a method and a `T[]` property can't be copied (copies don't make methods) and its typed read throws (review round 1, F7); an erased copy's object holding a `Box<any>` is taken as the member that leaves it out and a typed read of it throws (C169, H09b3), as does a class's instance holding an `any[]` read through a union member as a typed array (it keeps its class; review round 2's `e04`); objects a lie through `any` made throw at the typed read where Node goes on (D-L09), including reads that printed Node's value by accident on the base (a dynamic value of a string or object shares its pointer's encoding); an array of a subtype's elements read through a union member as its supertype's throws (above); a copy lists its properties in the member's declaration order, not the source's (C82's family, H22; review round 3's R3-1); a property of an object a union keeps as it is whose declared type differs from the member's is read through the site's slow path each time, converted, about 500 instructions a read where a cached read costs about 100 (R3-3); and a value of the member's wider type written to such a property throws where its own type doesn't hold it (`s.v = "six"` into a `number` field, D-073; R3-4).

### D-193 · Typed calls through `?.`, by an element access with a constant key, with object literal arguments for a union and with rest parameters find their instances; a method's optional call evaluates its object once (H09b3a); H09b3 split
- **Date:** 2026-10-20
- **Decision:** H09b3 splits into H09b3a (this: the call forms: `?.` calls, C179; element-access calls with a constant key, C185; object literal arguments for a parameter of a union of object types, C188; rest parameters, C170) and H09b3b (values: a generic method read as a value and passed on, `bind`, a generic value where a non-generic function type goes and the adapters that hide instances; C169's second half). No scope is dropped. H09b3a:
  - **`?.` calls** (C179). tsc resolves a call in an optional chain with a clone of its candidate signature flagged as the chain's (`getOptionalCallSignature`), whose return type has the chain's undefined (the outermost call) or its optional marker (a call inside the chain), and a generic call's resolved signature is an instantiation of that clone; so a demand's `want.Result` was `Box<number> | undefined`, which no instance returns, and the call took the erased copy, whose `Box<any>` threw (or, inside the chain, the marker's type didn't lower: PTS1001). `demandAt` takes the signature without the flags (`PtsWithoutCallChain`: a clone without them, for a generic call a re-instantiation of the unflagged candidate with the same mapper), so the demand is the one the call without `?.` makes; the outermost call (`PtsIsOuterCallChain`, `demand.chained`) converts its erased copy's result to the instance's result type (`fromErasedFor`), and the chain adds the undefined as before. A member read of a generic class instance inside a chain (`o?.make(5).value`, also of a method that isn't generic and returns a `Box<number>`, C195) takes the link's non-optional type (`receiverClass`, as `typeAt` does), where it was PTS1001 for every such read.
  - **A method's optional call evaluates its object once** (C203, pre-existing, found by the review). For `o.m?.(x)` the chain evaluated `o.m` to test it and the call's lowering evaluated `o` again, generic method or not (a receiver with an effect ran twice). `optionalChain` now evaluates the object of a method's optional call (`calledMethodReceiver`: `o.m?.(x)`, `o["m"]?.(x)`, not `super`'s, and not where the callee or its object is itself an optional chain) once into a local that the method's read and the call both use (`l.kept`), where its evaluation may have an effect (`mayHaveEffect`): not a name or `this`, whose second read can't be told apart, nor a member read naming a class, a namespace, a module or an enum, whose static member or function the call reaches without evaluating it as a value (`Util.m?.(x)`, `Math.max?.(…)`, `lib.f?.(x)` of a namespace import, `this.m?.(x)` in a static method).
  - **Element-access calls with a constant key** (C185). `o["m"](args)`, and `o[K](args)` for a const K initialised (through consts) with the string literal `"m"`, either perhaps asserted or parenthesized, where tsc's type of the key is that literal too (`constantKey`, `keyValue`), of a generic method lower as `o.m(args)` (`constantKeyCall`, `dottedCall`: a class's method, its instance or a dispatched demand; a static one; an interface's, an object literal's or a union's demand by name, `calleeName`), where the element access read the method as a value, a bound wrapper of the erased copy that lists no instances. A const key that isn't a local of the function being lowered is read after the object and before the arguments (`withKeyRead`: the object is kept in a local), so a key read before its declaration ran throws ReferenceError where JS does; a literal key isn't read. A key whose type alone says the name (a parameter, a `let`, a const declared of a literal type but initialised otherwise, an assertion of another string) may hold another string, since conversions from `any` don't check string literal types: such calls keep the value path, as before. In the last fallback (`DeclaredInstances`) and for `o?.["m"]?.(args)` (the method's object only the chain's value has) too. Methods that aren't generic keep the value path.
  - **Object literal arguments for a union of object types** (C188). An argument whose object provides a member of a union of object types as it is (`providedMember`), holds no dynamic value (`verifiesObjects`) and doesn't leave out a property holding one that another member declares (`leavesOut`), passes to the instance as it is (`passesAsIs`): `toUnion` takes it as that member, so `Ops.orElse<number>({ tag: "some", value: 3 }, 0)` reaches the instance with the object the erased copy gets boxed.
  - **Rest parameters** (C170). D-148 made no demand for a signature with a rest parameter. A rest parameter of the instance's elements now stands for the erased copy's (`isErasedRest`: `...value[]`, or `...Opt<any>[]` for `...xs: Opt<T>[]`): a value or dispatched call passes the instance the call's own new array of typed elements and the erased copy a checked copy of it, which nothing else holds (`erasedArguments`); a call by name lowers its arguments once as they are (`argumentList`) and passes the instance a new array of the rest arguments that are each one of its elements as they are (`restAsIs`), and makes no demand for a dynamic element or a spread (D-191). Not in the last fallback, which makes what the base made. D-095's inference from the result (`resultTypeArguments`) checks rest arguments against the rest's element type (a spread against the rest's type) instead of skipping such signatures, so `return errs({ kind: "empty" })` where a `Result<number, ParseError>` goes compiles as `err(…)` does.
- **Reason:** C170, C179, C185 and C188's acceptance: each form finds the instance its call without `?.`, its dotted call or its call with a variable finds. The reproducer's 496-case matrix (12 call forms × 8 demand kinds × 4 result shapes × 4 type arguments): 208 OK and 288 TypeErrors on the base, 496 OK with the change, nothing else moved; the RED tests fail on the base and match Node; D-155's lies through `any`, calls through `any` and D-191's dynamic arguments still keep the erased copy (the no-diff guard). C195 and C203 are pre-existing defects in the same `?.` lowering that the slice's forms reach.
- **Alternatives rejected:**
  - Letting `fitsInstance` take a result covariant with the chain's `Box | undefined`: calls inside a chain would still see the optional marker, and every instance call would need a conversion of its result.
  - Trusting a key's string literal type (the reproducer's prototype, review round 1's F3): `m[s as "id"](2)`, `const k: "id" = anyValue` and a parameter `key: "id"` given an `any` called `id` where Node calls the method the string names, silently.
  - Reading a key with `discard`: `isPure` takes a global's or a captured local's read for one without effects, so `m[LATE](…)` before `LATE`'s declaration called the method where JS throws ReferenceError.
  - Keeping every method's object in a local (review round 2's R2-1): a class's, `Math`'s, a namespace import's and a static `this` can't be lowered as values, so `Util.twice?.(3)` and the like, which compiled on the base, were PTS1001 in every build.
  - Routing `o?.["m"]?.(args)` (round 1's F1): the dotted lowering evaluated the object again as a nullable value, which failed to lower, and the whole program fell back to D-148's instances.
  - Rebuilding a by-name call's rest elements from the boxed values of the erased copy's rest (the prototype): elements the erased copy takes as objects of its own union (`Opt<any>`), not boxed, made no demand (review round 1's F11).
  - Moving H09b2's tests off `Ops["wrap"](7)` to another form that keeps the erased copy only until a later slice (a method value, a parenthesized callee): an `any` argument keeps it by design (D-191).
- **Supersedes:** D-148's exclusion of rest parameters from demands and D-095's of rest signatures; D-191's known gaps "a call through an element access keeps the erased copy (C185)" and "`?.` calls of every demand kind keep the erased copy (C179)"; D-192's C188.
- **Known gaps:** a key whose value isn't known to be the method's name (a parameter, a `let`, a union of literals `t[key]` with `key: "make" | "wrap"`, an element `t[keys[1]]`) reads the method as a value and keeps the erased copy (C196, H09b3b with method values); a parenthesized element-access callee `(m["make"])(5)` keeps it too, and `(o.m)?.(x)` is PTS1001 (C197, H29 with C149); `o?.["m"]?.(x)` keeps it, `o?.m?.(x)` is PTS1001 (or for a non-nullable o evaluates o twice) and `u.m?.(x)` through a union of object types is PTS1001, as on the base (C202, H29); a computed key of a method's optional call through `any` (`a[k()]?.(x)`) is evaluated twice (C206, H29); an optional argument that may be undefined is evaluated before the call's object (`recv().g(maybe())`, `inTemporaries`; C207, H29); a generic value with a tuple rest parameter (`<T extends unknown[]>(...xs: T) => Box<T>`) is PTS1001 (`rest parameters of type value`, C198, H29); a spread of a typed array into an `any[]` rest parameter is PTS1001 anywhere (C199, H29), so a call by name with a spread into a rest makes no demand and may not compile; a value call or a call by name whose result goes where a union of object types goes and whose instance makes another representation (D-095) is PTS1001 or keeps the erased copy, while the same call through `?.` finds the instance, since the outermost call of a chain takes the instance's result type before the chain's conversion (C200, H29); a static method's call before its class's declaration ran doesn't throw ReferenceError, dotted or by a key (C201, H29; a const key is read first there); `super[K](await x)` and `C[K](await x)` are PTS1001, as on the base (C204, H29); a call by name with a `yield` in its arguments keeps the erased copy, dotted or by a key, since the suspension keeps its method as a value (C205, H09b3b); calls inside erased code through an erased receiver keep the erased copy in every form, as D-148's; a call passing a dynamic value (an `any` argument, an `any` rest element) keeps it by design (D-191); generated C grows with the instances now used (the reproducer measured 8–28% on the new tests).

### D-194 · Wrappers of generic values forward their instances; a generic value where a non-generic function type goes is adapted by a demand; element-access keys find the method they name at run time; dynamic primitive arguments pass checked (H09b3b)
- **Date:** 2026-10-20
- **Decision:** H09b3b, the second slice of H09b3 (D-193): C169's second half, C196 and C205, with the pre-existing defects its reproducer, review and parent found in the same paths (C208–C211). Every new instance is made under `AllInstances` only (D-191's fallbacks), outside erased code, and what can't be made is taken back so that the build doesn't fall back.
  - **Wrappers forward their instances** (`pts/lower/forwarding.go`). A generic method read as a value is a closure bound to its object (D-044's `boundMethod`); `bind` without bound arguments makes another of a function value; `functionAsMethod` adapts an arrow or shorthand property where an interface declares a generic method (`const m: Maker = { make: <T>(x: T) => new Box(x) }`, `{ make }`). Each is a closure of a function of its own, whose descriptor listed no instances, so a typed call of it (a value call through a generic function type, D-148, or a call by name, D-191) took the erased copy, whose `Box<any>` didn't convert. Such a wrapper is now an erased value with a *forwarder* (`erasedValue.forward`, `addForwarder`). `specialize` pairs it with the demands that may call it (`reaches`: value demands of its erased function type, or for `functionAsMethod`'s adapter, demands by the method's name through a type its literal is assignable to, or its shape). `forwardingInstance` makes, per demand signature, a function that runs with the wrapper's closure (`ir.Func.Shares`, the same `Captures`, as D-190's instances do) and makes the wrapper's call for that signature as a demand of its own: on the function value it wraps (`valueForward`: `bind`'s and the adapter's), or by name on the object it is bound to (`methodForward`: through an interface or a literal's shape, D-191; for a class's generic method, a dispatched demand, D-101, so an override is found). A rest parameter passes on as its array (`erasedArguments`). If the forwarded call doesn't lower, it is taken back (`takeBack`, below) and the wrapper lists no instance for that signature, so the call keeps the wrapper's own function, as before. A method value's typed call follows D-148's value-call rule (an `any` argument is converted with a check and finds the instance), where the same method's call by name keeps D-191's (an `any` argument keeps the erased copy).
  - **A generic value where a non-generic function type goes** (`instantiatingAdapter`, `instantiatingAdapterSkipping`). A value of a generic function type (its IR type the erased copy's, D-078) given where a function type that isn't generic goes is adapted to it by an adapter whose call is a value demand on the value, for the type arguments tsc infers relating the value's signature to the context's (`PtsInstantiateSignatureInContextOf`, as `genericValue` did for declarations named there), else the value's call with its result converted with a check, as before: in `adapt` (typed declarations, returns, arguments of the program's functions, properties, elements, `as`, `| undefined`), for array callback methods (`callbackMethodCall`, which take a callback as it is), `Array.from`'s mapper, Promise callbacks, an instantiation expression `f<number>` of a value (its instantiated type is f's for those type arguments), and `functionAsMethod` for a method that isn't generic (the adapter takes `this` first and leaves it out). Only where the context's result type names the instance's (`namesResult`: the same type, or a union with it as a member, as a promise callback's `T | PromiseLike<T>`, an array and a readonly array of one element type counting as the same): a context that drops the result (`forEach`, `=> void`), takes `unknown` or `any`, or wants another or a wider type gains nothing from the instance, and the instance's types would make generic class instances the program never makes, whose bodies may not lower outside any trial (review round 3's R3-1). The adapter is built tentatively: if anything in it reports (its context's result is one the instance's doesn't convert to as it is, `(x: number) => Box<number | string>`), it is taken back as a failed trial's code is, with the functions lowered in it (its arguments' adapters) and the demands and erased values made there, and the report counter that trials read (`tentatively`, `takeBack`; `quietly` restores it too), and the plain adapter is made, as before (review round 1's F2: a report there sent the whole build to the fallback; round 2's R2-4 and R2-5: the nested adapters left behind failed to compile, and an instance holding such an adapter was dropped by its trial). A function-local const whose declared type isn't generic (`const t: (x: number) => Box<number> = <T>(x: T) => …`) is no longer one that only its calls by name meet (`calledOnlyName`).
  - **Element-access keys found at run time** (C196; `element_calls.go:keyedCall`, `keyedInstanceCall`). `o[key](args)` where the key's type is string literals each naming a generic method of o's type (a class's, an interface's or a literal's) whose result may hold generic class instances, and the key's value isn't known to be one name (a parameter, a `let`, an element, a union), is a demand by the name the key holds at run time: `ir.InstanceCall.ByKey`, which the C emitter calls with `pts_method_instance_find_keyed` (`object.c`: the class chain's entries of that name, else the object literal's method closure's, comparing whole names, caching nothing). Demands are made for every generic method of o's type with the same erased signature, not only those the key's type names, so a key holding another name (through `any` or an assertion, which conversions don't check) finds the instance of the method it names, as JS calls that method. Without an entry, or for a key or object held as a dynamic value (a `yield`'s), the call reads the member the key names (`DynamicGet`) and calls it as before. The object, then the key, then the arguments are evaluated; the method is found at the call. With a suspension in the arguments the object and the key are kept and the method is found after the resume (`isSettledCallee`'s `namesMethods`: a key whose type names only methods, which no code can assign).
  - **Dynamic primitive arguments pass checked** (C205; `passedAsIs`, `typedPrimitive`). D-191 passes no dynamic value to an instance by name. A dynamic value that tsc types as a primitive or a union of them (a `yield`'s resumed value, which a generator holds dynamically; `0 + (yield 1)`) now passes converted with a check to the instance's parameter, as a direct call of a class's generic method already converts it; an `any` and an `unknown` argument keep the erased copy, as before. C205's cause was this, not the suspension (the reproducer's `p06b`).
  - **Pre-existing defects in these paths.** A dispatched (abstract or overridden) generic method read as a value called the static type's method, so an override didn't run (C208), and an abstract one's value made an instance of its bodiless declaration, PTS9001 in every context (C209): its value now calls the receiver's class's method by name (`genericMethodOf`), as a dispatched call does. `o.m.call(x, …)`, `o.m.apply(x, …)` and `o.m.bind(x)` of a class's generic method ran it on `o`, silently, where JS runs it on `x` (C210): they now run it on x where the method may use its object (`this` or `super` in its parameters' defaults or body, arrow functions included but not the functions, classes and literals' methods it declares: `usesObject`; or it is abstract or overridden: `mayUseThis`), which then converts x to the class (with a check from `any`, else D-044's PTS1001, where JS would run it on an object of another type); a method that can't use its object runs on its own, as before, which no program can tell apart (`runsOnThisArg`; review round 1's F1, round 2's R2-2 and R2-3). An abstract or overridden one follows D-044's rule for class methods, the method of x's class running on x, as non-generic methods do (round 2's R2-1: the base ran o's method on o, which is what JS does only for a method that doesn't use `this`), except that given no x, or one tsc types null, undefined or void, it runs its own object's method, as JS runs the method it read without an object, which only a method that doesn't use `this` survives (round 3's R3-2). Destructuring a class's method from an instance was PTS1001, generic or not (C211): in a declaration's or a parameter's pattern it binds the method to the instance, as `o.m` does (D-044), an override dispatched.
- **Reason:** C169's, C196's and C205's acceptance: each typed call finds the instance of its signature (for C196 the method the key names at run time, for C205 after the resume) or keeps an honest diagnostic. The reproducer's 575-case matrix (method values, held, bound, in fields, returned, through `?.`, destructured, by run-time keys, after suspensions, and `call`/`apply`, × 12 definition kinds × 4 consumers × 5 result shapes): HEAD 136 OK / 378 TypeErrors / 48 PTS9001 / 13 PTS1001; with the change 540 OK / 28 TypeErrors (the D-155 lies, and C212's assertions) / 7 PTS1001 (C218), none worse; the H09 754-case matrix: HEAD 562 OK / 116 TypeErrors / 2 PTS1001, with the change 678 OK / 0 TypeErrors / the same 2 PTS1001 (D-044's `bind` of an object type's method to another object; the 74 TSERR are its generator's own `.call`/`.apply` cases, as on HEAD); four review rounds, the last "ready"; the skipped `run/generic_method_factories.pts` runs. Forwarding keeps D-078's one representation per function value (the wrapper is still the value; its instances run with its closure) and D-148's run-time lookup, so identity, `name`, `length`, `console.log`, `any` and the D-155 checks are unchanged; a forwarding instance's call is a demand like any other, so trials, the fallbacks and the instance limit apply to what it reaches.
- **Alternatives rejected:**
  - Forwarding instance lookups at run time through a wrapper's descriptor (the lookup following the wrapper to its receiver or wrapped closure): a value entry takes (closure, arguments) and a method's entry (closure, object, arguments), so a value call can't call what a by-name lookup finds, and an adapter's parameters aren't the instance's.
  - The context's instance itself as an inline generic arrow's value in a non-generic context (as `genericValue` does for declarations): a second representation of one function value (D-190), and it covers neither values held in variables nor method values.
  - A forwarder of a class's non-dispatched method calling the method's instance directly (review round 1's F5 suggestion): it saves about a sixth of the C per pair (the class's entry and table row and the run-time lookup), while the pairs themselves, C172's, are the cost.
  - A switch over the key's literal names to the dotted calls (the reproducer's alternative for C196): each branch lowers the arguments again, and a key that holds another name falls to the value path, the erased copy's `Box<any>`.
  - Demands for the names the key's type says only: a key holding another name through `any` or an assertion found no entry and threw, where JS calls the method the string names (H09b3a review's `f1_lying_key`, `e02_as_key`).
  - A PTS1001 for keyed calls of generic methods: a program that compiled, and whose call may never run, would stop compiling.
  - Converting a `yield`'s resumed value to its tsc type where it is used: `console.log(yield 1)` resumed by `next()` prints undefined, as in JS, and would throw (the reproducer's `p06c`); only an instance by name needs a typed value, and a direct call converts it already.
  - Passing any dynamic value tsc types other than `any` to an instance (D-191's rejected fifth snapshot): an object an erased copy made may be one of another representation; a primitive is checked as it is.
  - Running a class's generic method on any thisArg (review round 1's snapshot): `m.id.call(undefined, 5)`, which compiled and ran on the base and in JS for a method that doesn't use `this`, became PTS1001 (F1); on a thisArg typed as an instance of its class (round 2's snapshot): a structural twin's object was PTS1001 for a method that doesn't use `this` (R2-2). Running it on its own object always (the base): silently wrong for a method that uses `this`.
  - Keeping an overridden generic method that doesn't use `this` on its own object (round 2's R2-1 suggestion): which overrides use `this` takes every subclass, and a generic class's instances are made only as the program's code needs them; D-044's rule is the one class methods already follow.
- **Supersedes:** D-191's known gap "an arrow property where an interface declares a method ... is wrapped in a `functionAsMethod` adapter, whose closure lists no instances (C169's adapters, H09b3)"; D-190's and D-148's known gaps "generic methods read as values and `bind`, generic values given where a non-generic function type goes (`[1, 2].map(mk)`, an adapter that hides the instances)" (C169); D-193's known gaps C196 and C205 and its "a key whose type alone says the name ... keep[s] the value path" for keys of generic methods whose results may hold generic class instances; D-044's PTS1001 for a class's method read from a declaration's or parameter's pattern and, for classes' generic methods, `call`/`apply`/`bind` that ran on `o` rather than thisArg.
- **Known gaps:** `f.call(x, …)`, `f.apply(x, […])` and `f.bind(x, a)` (with bound arguments) of a generic function or method: tsc infers their type parameters as `unknown`, so their results are `Box<unknown>`, as in pts; asserting one as `Box<number>` is a checked cast between instances of other type arguments, which throws `TypeError: Expected Box<number>, got Box<any>` where Node goes on, as `new Box<unknown>(5) as Box<number>` does on the base (C212, intentional with D-078 and D-154; an instantiation expression `(make<number>).call(…)` finds its instance). An adapter whose context's result isn't the instance's (`(x: number) => Box<number | string>`, `(x: any) => Box<number>`, `(x: number) => Box<number | undefined>`, where tsc infers the type arguments from the parameters, or `=> unknown`, whose value the program then asserts `as Box<number>`, or a result that only the checker's identity relation, not the type's own identity, makes the instance's: an aliased union, an object holding the box, an async `then` callback's `Promise<Box<T>>` against `TResult | PromiseLike<TResult>`; review round 4's R4-2) keeps the erased copy, a TypeError at its call or assertion as on the base (C215, H29); so does a keyed call whose key a generator holds as a dynamic value (`m[yield 1](5)`, C216, H29). A lying key that names a generic method whose result holds no generic class instance keeps the base's value path, which finds only the methods the key's type names in method tables: TypeError where Node calls the method (C217, H29). `bind` and `call` of an object type's method with another object as `this` stay D-044's PTS1001; so does running a class's method that uses `this` on an object of another type (an object literal, an interface value, another instance of its generic class), where JS reads that object. A class whose arrow field implements an interface's method is PTS1001 (C213, H29); `C[key](x)` with a key naming static methods is PTS1001 (the class as a value, C218, H29); destructuring a method in an assignment pattern (`({ m } = o)`) stays PTS1001 (C219, H29); a method value's `length` doesn't count an optional parameter (C214, H29). Code grows with wrappers × demands of their erased type, as values × demands did (C172, H09b5): a forwarding instance, two entries and the method's instance per pair; 60 method values each passed to its own one-type consumer (review round 1's `s_60`): 10,461 → 93,741 lines of C and a 1.4 → 32.9 s build (607 MB), where 60 declarations so passed make 43,942 lines (7.4 s) on the base and with the change; the test corpus's C is unchanged but for the new tests and three existing ones (≤ 1.4%). A keyed lookup is uncached (3M calls: 153–343 ms against 78–125 ms dotted, review round 1). The demands this slice adds (adapters, forwarding instances, keyed calls) pair as every value demand does (D-190), so they are new ways into C176's cliff: an adapter's demand pairs with an unrelated generic arrow whose class instance for the demand's types doesn't lower, which no trial takes back, and the build falls back (review round 4's `t04`; a plain typed call does the same on the base, `t04b`; H09b5).

### D-195 · Code that an instance's type arguments make dead lowers as a trap; never type arguments return what they have (H09b4a); H09b4 split
- **Date:** 2026-10-20 (review round 1's fixes 2026-10-21)
- **Decision:** H09b4 splits into H09b4a (this: code that an instance's type arguments make dead, C171, and never type arguments, C194) and H09b4b (narrowings that tsc itself types as never or as an intersection, in plain code as in generic code, C220; branded primitives, C221; `in` narrowing to a property the type lacks, C222; discriminants narrowed through a type parameter's union constraint, C223; an instance's optional chain narrowed by a predicate, C226: all pre-existing, found by H09b4's reproducer and reviewer). No scope is dropped. H09b4a:
  - **Dead code is a trap** (`pts/lower/dead_code.go`). A read of a variable, property or tuple element (`x`, `o.v`, `t[0]`; not inside an optional chain, whose own undefined isn't the property's) is dead in an instance where no value of its declared type has the type of the tests that narrow it: tsc's narrowed type without the members of its intersections that mention the instance's type parameters (`testedType`: `T & number` and `T["a"] & number` → number, `keyof T` as it is; `null` where tsc narrows a `T extends string | null` through the constraint), intersected with the declared type for the instance (`isDeadRead`; `PtsIntersectionType`, reduced as tsc reduces it where used, `PtsReducedType`). tsc keeps a narrowing across what it doesn't follow (a closure's assignment, a call that changes a property, an await, a getter), so the value may have changed since, but never to one its declared type doesn't have: stores are typed and checked. The intersection has no values (`uninhabited`) if it is never, or has two disjoint members, of different value kinds (primitive, array, function, class instance) or instances of different classes, neither assignable to the other (`string & number`, `number & any[]`, `string & Function`, `Pt & Other`, `Uint8Array & any[]`), though tsc's narrowed and declared types intersect in the generic code (code tsc already types as never, an exhaustive switch's `assertNever(s)`, lowers as before) and the declared type has values for the instance (a `never[]`'s element, of type never, is undefined). A unit of code holding a dead read where it starts, with nothing that narrows in between (`evaluatedFirst`: a statement's own expressions, not its nested statements and functions, the branches of `?:`, the right operands of `&&`, `||`, `??` and the logical assignments, an optional chain past its `?.`, a loop's body and update, a switch's cases), can't run: a statement (`statementOrTrap`), a branch of `?:` (`sameType`), the right operand of `&&` or `||` (`logicalWithDeadRight`, whose result has the whole's union type where that holds the left's) or `??`, and an arrow's expression body (`functionExpressionBody`) lower as `throw TypeError("code that can't run for T = string ran: `x` narrows to `T & number` here; a type predicate, assertion or cast was wrong")`, a void call that converts to the other branch's type. The statements after a dead one in its list lower to nothing but their hoisted function declarations' bodies and their variables, never initialized, which closures made before them or in a later case may capture (`statementList`, `uninitialized`). A dead read where any value goes as it is doesn't make its unit a trap, and reads the value it holds as before (`takesAnyValueAsIs`): where its context's type is any or unknown (`String`, `JSON.stringify` and `console` arguments, elements and properties of literals there, `unknown` parameters, `unknown[]`'s push, also through an array literal that is spread), `typeof`, a template's placeholder, an operand of `+` with a string or of `===`/`!==`; not a copy (`const y = x`), whose type is the narrowed one that later code reads, nor a spread of the read, which iterates it.
  - **For every type argument that runs the instance.** Instances are told apart by IR types (a function's also by literal type arguments), so `Holder<Id>`'s methods, for a branded string Id, are `Holder<unknown>`'s. `isDeadRead` tests with each type argument replaced by the widest type of its IR type (`deadTest`, `widestOfIRType`): unknown where that IR type is a dynamic value's (a branded primitive, a promise, a recursive union such as `Json`; `isDynamicIRType`), a literal's or enum's base type, the argument itself where it holds no literal types and no types of dynamic IR type but any and unknown (`literalFree`, which asks no IR types of the argument's parts, since a phantom type argument's would make class instances: in type arguments, properties, index signatures and call signatures, `() => 1` sharing `() => number`'s IR type, `{ v: Json }` `{ v: unknown }`'s, a union that holds itself being dynamic; a class of the program or the library is its own IR type), unknown otherwise. So code is a trap only where it is dead for all of them. An object type argument with a literal discriminant (`Ci`, whose IR type `{ kind: string, r: number }` a `{ kind: "xx", r: number }` shares) or that holds a union that recurs through itself has no traps: its code lowers as before, which for a dead narrowed region is the base's PTS1001 (C223).
  - **Live narrowings tsc doesn't reduce.** In an instance, an intersection of a type parameter with other types (`T & any[]` after Array.isArray) is, for each member of the parameter's type argument, that member where the other types include it (`number[]` or `readonly number[]`, where tsc keeps `number[] & any[]`; an array includes an array whose elements take its own, `includes`), nothing where no value of it is left (`string & any[]` for a `string | number[]`) and the intersection otherwise (`narrowedInstance`, in `instantiate`). `x instanceof C` for a primitive x is false, and `===`/`!==` between a primitive and a primitive of another kind or an object (`x === 1` for a string x) is a constant unless an operand is void, whose value JS keeps (`isPrimitive`, `neverStrictlyEqual`): tests whose narrowing is dead, which tsc rejects in plain code.
  - **`narrowsAway` is gone.** It kept the erased copy for a value or method demand whose instance narrowed away (D-148): such instances now lower, and trials (D-190) still take back one that fails.
  - **never type arguments (C194).** An element read of type never (an element of a `never[]`, an instance's for never) can only be missing: it is undefined, as in JS (D-015's path for elements that may be undefined). A function's result that is never only for its instance (`firstPlain<never>`'s `T`) is a dynamic value rather than void ("only throws" is for a result declared never), and so is an arrow's whose expression body, not a call, tsc types never though it has a value (`() => firstValue([]).value ?? "fallback"`).
  - **Upstream:** `internal/checker/pts.go` exports `PtsIntersectionType` and `PtsReducedType` (`docs/FORK.md`).
- **Reason:** C171's and C194's acceptance: instances and direct calls run as Node. The reproducer's 633-case matrix (23 narrowing forms, 40 dead-region shapes, 17 call forms, 12 type arguments, controls, never): HEAD 248 OK / 144 TypeErrors / 17 RangeErrors / 185 PTS1001 / 39 PTS9001; with the change 532 OK / 6 TypeErrors (four lies, D-155's checked `any` twice) / 1 RangeError / 74 PTS1001 / 20 PTS9001, none OK → not OK; what remains is H09b4b's (plain code, brands, `in`, constraint discriminants), local classes (H29), the lies below, D-155's checked `any` and D-085's RangeError. Review round 1 found that tsc's narrowed type alone made stale narrowings (F1), type arguments sharing an IR type by other routes (F2: function types, recursive unions) and an optional chain's undefined (F5) reach the trap with no lie, that the dead tail left out variables closures capture (F3), and that value-agnostic uses of a lie's value trapped (F4): each is fixed and tested. Round 2 found the same holes through indexed-access types (`T["a"] | number`, R2-F1) and dynamic types nested in type arguments (R2-F2), and two new failures of round 1's fixes (`number[] & string[]` taken as `number[]`, R2-F3; a dead spread let through, R2-F4): fixed and tested. Round 3 found that asking for the IR types of a type argument's parts made class instances for phantom type arguments, whose code may not lower (R3-F1): `literalFree` is structural now; round 4 that it must walk index signatures too (`{ [k: string]: J2 }`, R4-F1); round 5 found it ready.
- **Alternatives rejected:**
  - A trap wherever tsc narrows to never (the reproducer's prototype): an exhaustive switch's `assertNever(s)` reached by a lie and a lying predicate's branch that prints the value, which run in Node and on the base, became traps (4 regressions).
  - tsc's narrowed type as it is (the fix commit): it keeps narrowings across what it doesn't follow, so code with no lie reached the trap (review round 1, F1).
  - Erasing the instance's type parameters in the narrowed type to unknown: `keyof T` becomes `keyof unknown`, never, so reads of keys (`o[key]` for a `key: keyof T`) became traps (two corpus programs and `logical_assign_erased` in the suite on round 1's fixes).
  - Lowering dead reads as dynamic values (the reproducer's suggestion): types tsc infers from the narrowed value (`new Box(x)`'s `Box<never>`, a conditional's `Box<never> | Box<string>`) still don't lower, so the region still needs a trap.
  - Lowering a unit tentatively and making it a trap only if it fails: a unit makes closures, instances and class instances that only a trial can take back, and a unit that lowers keeps code whose types no value has (`number[] & string`'s length read from an array: PTS9001).
  - Leaving regions that a type predicate or assertion function narrows as they were (they may lie): honest predicates in generic helpers are the common case, and their instances failed (C171).
  - Telling instances apart by the reads they make dead: value and method demands find instances by IR signature at run time, so two instances of one signature can't be told apart there.
  - Letting a copy (`const y = x`) of a dead read through (review round 1's suggestion): y's type is the narrowed one, which later code reads (`y.length` of a never), so a dead branch with no lie stopped compiling.
- **Intentional:** a type predicate, assertion function or cast that lies lets code run that an instance's type arguments make dead; where it uses the value as its narrowed type (`x + 1`, a copy), it throws the trap's TypeError, where Node runs it with the value as it is (the base rejected `x + 1` with PTS1001, and ran a copy that only prints), as D-067's lying guards throw; where it uses it as any value, it runs as Node. Documented in SPEC §8 and the GUIDE.
- **Supersedes:** D-148's "Instances that wouldn't lower" (`narrowsAway`) and D-190's known gap "D-102's narrowed branches" (C171). D-102's reason for not monomorphizing every generic const (instances of functions that test their type parameters' values don't type-check) no longer holds; the decision stands.
- **Known gaps:** C220–C223, C226 and C227 (H09b4b). Type arguments of one IR type share an instance lowered with the first one's types (C177, H09b5): where the widest type of an argument's IR type is unknown (a branded primitive, a promise), its narrowed code isn't a trap, so `arrays<Promise<number>>` and `arrays<unknown>` with an `Array.isArray(x)` branch are PTS1001, as on the base. D-015's "where any value can go" misses `unknown` and `any` parameters, results and `unknown[]` literals (C224, H11). A void value compared with `===` is PTS1001 (C225, H29).
- **Tests:** `run/generic_narrowed_dead_branches.pts`, `generic_narrowed_dead_regions.pts`, `generic_narrowed_dead_branch_direct_calls.pts`, `generic_narrowed_dead_branch_values.pts`, `generic_narrowed_intersections.pts`, `generic_narrowed_live_controls.pts`, `generic_never_type_arguments.pts`, `generic_never_results.pts` (the reproducer's), `generic_dead_branches_shared_instances.pts`, `generic_narrowed_stale_narrowings.pts`, `generic_dead_tail_bindings.pts`, `generic_dead_reads_any_value.pts`, `generic_narrowed_library_types.pts`, `generic_narrowed_dead_spreads.pts`, `narrowed_never_branches_that_run.pts`, `generic_instances_dead_branches.pts` (renamed from `generic_instances_that_dont_lower.pts`, now against Node), `generic_factories_fallbacks.pts` (against Node), `generic_never_value_call.pts` (unskipped), `generic_dead_branch_lies.pts` (no-diff); skipped for H09b4b: `narrowed_dead_branches_plain.pts`, `branded_primitives.pts`, `in_narrowing_absent_member.pts`, `generic_narrowed_constraint_discriminants.pts`; `lower_test.go`'s `TestCodeThatAnInstancesTypeArgumentsMakeDeadLowersAsATrap`; `TestAGenericArrowsInstanceThatDoesntLowerIsDroppedWithWhatItMade` and `driver_test.go`'s two fallback tests make their failing instance with `==` between a string and a number, since dead narrowing now lowers.

### D-196 · Narrowed reads that their IR types don't hold read the values they hold: branded primitives, `in`, constraint discriminants, narrowed optional chains, array intersections, stale narrowings (H09b4b1); H09b4b split
- **Date:** 2026-10-21
- **Decision:** H09b4b splits into H09b4b1 (this: live narrowings whose type the read's IR type doesn't hold: C221, C222, C223, C226, C227, and C220's stale narrowings) and H09b4b2 (C220's plain code that tsc narrows to never or to an intersection no value has, and generic code tsc already types never: traps where the narrowing can't be stale, `assertNever` and printing uses kept). No scope is dropped. H09b4b1:
  - **Branded primitives are their primitives** (C221). An intersection of one primitive type with object types that only name properties (`string & { __brand: "id" }`, `number & { readonly __unit: "usd" }`, `` `/${string}` & { … } ``, `string & {}`; `brandedPrimitive`), and a union of such intersections (`("up" | "down") & { __brand: "dir" }`, which tsc distributes; `unbranded`), is its primitive wherever the lowering asks for a checker type (`instantiate`, so `typeAt` and `irType`): its IR type, members, methods, operators, indexing, narrowing and conversions are the primitive's; `widestOfIRType` gives the primitive's base type (`Holder<Id>`'s instance is `Holder<string>`'s); an index signature keyed by a brand (`Record<UserId, User>`) is a record of its primitive's keys (`recordType`). A brand's own property is read through `any` as Node reads it (undefined).
  - **A property its object's type lacks is found at run time** (C222, C223). `x.k` after `"k" in x` for an x whose type has no k (an interface, an object type, a union of them, or a class that a subclass extends with k: `isMappedProperty` takes `Animal & Record<"bark", unknown>`), and a member that a discriminant narrowed through a type parameter's union constraint names (`x.side` for `T extends Sq | Ci` in the instance for Ci, where tsc types x as Sq, also for a union type argument narrower than the constraint), are read and written by name at run time with a check, as dynamic objects' members already were: `valueProperty` and `valuePlace` box the object (before, the C passed an unboxed object where a dynamic value goes: PTS9001), `shapeProperty` hands a union of objects or a class without the property to them, and patterns read such a property by name (`missingProperty`; PTS1001 before), as do object spreads and rest elements where it is the object's own (`missingOwnProperty`: `Object.hasOwn`, since a spread copies own properties only, and `in` also finds a class's getter or method on the prototype; undefined otherwise). A reference that tsc narrowed to a type its value's type doesn't provide converts where that type goes with a check, as a dynamic value does (`objectCast`, `isReferenceNarrowedTo`; PTS1001 before), keeping the object where its layout provides the type: the constraint's narrowed value (`const s: Sq = x`, an Sq argument, `new Box<Sq>(x)`), and a type predicate's intersection (`x` is an `A & B` after `isB(x)` for an `x: A`). The constraint's branch needs no trap: the narrowed type is a real member, and a cast that lies reaches it with the value it holds, as Node does.
  - **An optional chain narrowed to its `?.`'s undefined** (C226): where tsc narrows a chain to a type that doesn't hold its read's value (`o?.v === undefined`, `o?.v == null`, a predicate's `number | undefined` for a string v; plain code and instances alike), `optionalChainFrom` builds it in the read's type with undefined (`holds`) and converts it to the narrowed type with a check, also where a type with undefined goes (`const n: number | undefined = o?.v`), then to that type, which a stale narrowing's chain may hold the property's value in again, but not where every value goes as it is (any, unknown, `??`'s left: `takesEveryValue`). A chain whose object is a dynamic value may be undefined where tsc says it isn't (`x[0]?.length` of an x read dynamically) and holds undefined too.
  - **Stale narrowings** (C220's live part, and C230). tsc keeps a narrowing across what it doesn't follow (a call that sets a property, an await, a closure's assignment), so a second test can type a live value never, or a test's own operand the stale type. A union read that tsc types never, where it may have changed (a property, or a variable another function assigns), is its value boxed (`narrowed`), which its uses convert with a check, as other stale narrowings' already did (D-089, D-140); a read that can't have changed is unreachable code, as before. An operand of a test of what any value is (`typeof`'s, an equality's, instanceof's left, a switch's: `testsAnyValue`) that is such a read (`typeof o.v`, `typeof o["v"]`, `o.v instanceof P2`, `o.v === 5`) is what it holds, as a dynamic value, not converted to the narrowed type (which threw `Expected string, got number` or `The value narrowed to P1 is not an instance of it` where the narrowing was stale).
  - **Array intersections** (C227): `narrowedInstance` and `instantiate` take an intersection of array types (`T & string[]` for a T of `(string | number)[]` or `number[]`, from an honest `v is string[]`; `number[] & string[]` in plain code; `withoutArrayIntersection`) as the member whose elements every other member's take (`(string | number)[] & string[]` is a string[], as tsc narrows it in plain code), and as any otherwise (`number[] & string[]`, which only an array of no elements has; read dynamically). A library member whose receiver is a dynamic value that tsc types as the member's receiver converts it with a check (`builtinReceiver`: `x[0].length` where x is read dynamically), as does a dynamic value tsc types as an array where an array method is called (`arrayObject`); `for…of` iterates a typed array that tsc types as any (the instance's `number[] & string[]`) as a dynamic value.
- **Reason:** the acceptance of C221, C222, C223, C226 and C227 (each was PTS9001 or PTS1001; each now runs as Node), and C220's stale-narrowing part (PTS1001 `converting number | string to number`, now as Node). These are live code (no lie needed), unlike C220's plain dead code, which needs H09b4a's traps outside instances and a rule for which plain reads can't be stale: a separate slice.
- **Alternatives rejected:**
  - A trap for the constraint discriminant's dead branch (D-195's rule): the narrowed type is a member of the constraint, which lowers, so a lie reaches it with its value as in Node; a trap would make a lie throw for no gain.
  - `number[] & string[]` as the argument's `number[]` (H09b4a round 2's R2-F3): tsc types the generic code's `x[0]` as string, so a string member was read from a double (PTS9001).
  - `any` for every live array intersection: `(string | number)[] & string[]` read dynamically where plain code's string[] reads elements by their checks; mirroring plain code keeps one behaviour.
  - Brands as dynamic values (the base's IR type for intersections): every member read of the primitive failed in C.
  - Reading every test operand unnarrowed: a read that can't have changed (a `const`, a parameter only its function assigns) is what its narrowing says, and its narrowed IR type is cheaper.
  - The declared union, unconverted, for a test's operand (the first version): equality can't compare a `number | string` with a number (`o.n === 1` after `typeof o.n === "number"` was PTS1001), and any-typed contexts (`takesAnyValueAsIs`'s) reach conditional branches whose type tsc computes from the narrowed one (`user.age === undefined ? "?" : user.age` in a `console.log`).
- **Intentional:** pushing a string into a `number[]` that an honest `v is string[]` narrowed (an empty array) throws `TypeError: Cannot set property element of Array: expected number, got string`, where Node's untyped array takes it: PTS arrays are typed (D-155's checked writes). A lie that reaches a constraint discriminant's branch and passes its value where the narrowed type goes gets a checked conversion of it, which may be a copy (D-071), and throws D-070's TypeError where the object lacks the narrowed type's members (`{ kind: "sq", r: 1 } as unknown as Ci` reaching `const s: Sq = x`), where Node stores it. Writing a property that an object of a class lacks as a field (`x.e = 7` for `x: C & Partial<Extra>`; after `"bark" in a`, a subclass's method of that name) throws D-072's TypeError where Node adds or replaces it; these were PTS1001.
- **Known gaps:** H09b4b2 (C220's plain dead code; skipped `run/narrowed_dead_branches_plain.pts`, `generic_typed_never_dead_branches.pts`). An array of a union element type narrowed to an array of one member can't be aliased (`const y = x`) or have callback methods (`x.map((s) => s.length)`): PTS1001 in plain code and instances alike (pre-existing; C228 → H29; skipped `run/narrowed_array_aliases.pts`). A value narrowed to `Function` can't be called (C229 → H29, D-170's `Function` type; skipped `run/in_narrowing_absent_method.pts`). `in` on an array (`"len" in xs`) is PTS1001 (pre-existing). A brand's own property read directly (`id.__brand`) is PTS1001: it is undefined at run time, which its string type can't hold (C232 → H29). Pre-existing, found by the review: an `any` that Array.isArray narrows, holding a typed array, is copied at each use, so writes are lost (C231 → H22, high; skipped `run/any_isarray_mutations.pts`), and a subclass's instance where a union of classes goes is PTS1001 (C233 → H29). A spread's copy still lists a property it left out because the object holds it on its prototype (C234 → H29, low; skipped `run/in_narrowing_spread_prototype_members.pts`). After `"k" in x` on a union whose member declares k as another type, the read takes that member's type (C235 → H29, pre-existing; skipped `run/in_narrowing_union_member_types.pts`).
- **Costs:** a test's operand that may have changed is boxed and compared as a dynamic value whether or not the narrowing is stale (nothing tells them apart at compile time): in a micro-loop of `===` on a narrowed property, 10.6% more instructions (callgrind, review round 1's F10); other code is unchanged.
- **Tests:** `run/branded_primitives.pts`, `in_narrowing_absent_member.pts`, `generic_narrowed_constraint_discriminants.pts` (unskipped, extended), `narrowed_optional_chains.pts`, `stale_narrowings_never.pts`, `narrowed_array_intersections.pts` (the parent's), `array_type_intersections.pts`, `array_type_intersection_searches.pts`, `branded_primitives_uses.pts`, `generic_constraint_discriminant_forms.pts`, `generic_narrowed_stale_never_reads.pts`, `narrowed_stale_never_reads_plain.pts`, `narrowed_stale_test_operands.pts`, `in_narrowing_absent_member_routes.pts`, `optional_chains_narrowed_by_predicates.pts`, `narrowed_never_defaults_that_run.pts` (a guard; the reproducer's), and after review round 1 `narrowed_property_equality.pts` (a guard), `stale_narrowings_tested.pts`, `predicate_intersections_keep_objects.pts`, `in_narrowing_writes_and_copies.pts`, `generic_constraint_discriminant_writes.pts`, `branded_record_keys.pts`, after round 2 `class_mapped_intersections.pts`, against Node; `lower_test.go`'s `TestNarrowedReadsThatTheirIRTypesDontHoldReadTheirValues`; skipped `any_isarray_mutations.pts` (C231), `in_narrowing_spread_prototype_members.pts` (C234), `in_narrowing_union_member_types.pts` (C235).

### D-197 · Code tsc narrows to no value is a trap where kind tests made it and nothing else can reach it; otherwise it reads what it holds (H09b4b2)
- **Date:** 2026-10-21
- **Decision:** C220's rest. A read of a variable, property, getter or literal-keyed element (`readTypes`; `o[c.key]`'s key is no longer taken for the element's property) that tsc narrows to a type no value of its declared type has (`narrowsToNoValue`: never, or an intersection of disjoint kinds or classes such as `number & any[]` or `Pt & Other`, where the declared type has values), in plain code and in generic code tsc itself types so (`x: T & string` tested as a number), is one of three things:
  - **Dead, a trap** (`isDeadRead`; D-195's `trap`, `statementList`, `deadOperand`; the message names no type arguments: `TypeError: code that can't run ran: `x` narrows to `never` here; a type predicate, assertion or cast was wrong`), where it holds no value its tests didn't rule out (`mayHoldUntestedValue` false):
    - it can't be stale: not a property, element or getter, not a variable another function assigns (D-140), not a variable assigned after its declaration and read in a nested function, which may run while its own function awaits or yields (`mayBeStale`);
    - it isn't a loose local (D-090, D-172), which may be undefined;
    - no value of its declared type's IR type passes the tests either (`noValueOfItsIRTypePasses`): for never, tsc's flow analysis is run again from the widest type of that IR type (`widestOfIRType`, now number or string for an enum; the upstream export `PtsFlowTypeOfReference`), so an enum's `=== null` or a literal union's `typeof … === "number"` is dead, and an exhausted switch over an enum, which holds any number with no cast, isn't;
    - kind tests narrowed it, not tests of its value against others (`testsValues`, `narrowing_tests.go`, which follows tsc's flow graph back from the read, through labels and closures' starts): an equality other than with null, undefined or a `typeof`, a switch other than on `typeof` or `true`, or a property's truthiness, that mentions the variable, one its pattern binds beside it, or a const that aliases part of it in the two forms tsc narrows through (`const kind = a.kind`, an access; `const { kind } = a`, destructuring a variable or an access: `aliasedPart`), also through const conditions (`const isAdd = kind === "add"`). Such tests narrow through literal and enum types whose IR types hold other values without a lie: an enum's numbers, a class's literal field that its base class's method writes (`kind: "add"` renamed through `Base.rename`), an Error subclass's `name` written through `Error`, a discriminant of a destructured sibling or alias, `code === expected` for an enum `expected`, an object `Object.assign` fills from JSON. Comparing the variable itself with a literal (`x === "skip"`, an enum member, a switch on it with literal cases) or a boolean variable with true and false is a kind test: the re-run from the widest type covers its literal types.
  - **What it holds**, boxed and converted with a check where it is used (`narrowed`, `readsAsItsDeclaredType`), where it may hold an untested value, for every declared type now (D-196's was unions), but as its declared type where it is an element's key and that type's IR type is a number or a string (`names[c]` for an enum's other number: it holds a value of its declared IR type; `isKeyOfItsType`), boxed where it is a union (`Kind | "none"`, found at run time); and also where a dead read is lowered outside the part of its unit that runs first (a `for` loop's update or later conditions, a `do…while` condition, a case label, past `?.`), in plain code and in instances (`isDeadRead` in `narrowed`), rather than failing. An intersection no value has is `any` to the lowering (`instantiate`), so a stale `o.v.length` after `typeof o.v === "number"` and `Array.isArray(o.v)` is read at run time.
  - **Its declared type, as before**, where it goes where its declared type goes (`goesAsItsDeclaredType`: a destination declared never, `assertNever(s)` or `const u: never = s`, or a generic signature's parameter declared never, but not a never tsc inferred, `new Box(s)`'s; or a context whose type takes the read's declared type: `return x` from a function of x's type, `const y: string = x`, `x as string`, an argument of its type, a push into its array), and for a dead read also where any value goes (D-195's `takesAnyValueAsIs`). A lie that reaches such a use gets the value as in Node.
- **Also:** a type predicate's argument is a test of what it holds (`testsAnyValue`, C230's rule; `isPredicateArgument`, the upstream export `PtsTypePredicateParameterIndex`): `isCi(o.v)` where a stale narrowing typed `o.v` Sq reads what it holds, as `typeof o.v` does, instead of converting it to Sq with a check (C236).
- **Reason:** C220's acceptance (plain code and generic code tsc types never lower, as a trap or as the value they hold, and `assertNever`, never consts, casts back and printing keep running): H09b4's 633-case matrix 553 → 617 OK, the reproducer's 2,046-case matrix 1,422 → 1,849 OK (meeting the plan, a lie's trap counted where its value is used as the narrowed type: 1,446 → 1,987), every program of the run, errors, corpus and docs suites emits the same C but the fixed ones; corpus `functional/reactive_signals.pts` now prints what Node prints. The rule's conditions each answer a way code tsc types never runs in Node without a lie, which the reproducer and review round 1 found.
- **Alternatives rejected:**
  - "Can't be stale" alone (the plan's wording, the reproducer's starting point and the fix commit): enums, literal unions, loose locals (the reproducer's guards), and class unions, destructured discriminants and enum equalities (review round 1's F1) reach code tsc types never with no lie.
  - `literalFree` of the declared type for never (the fix commit): it passes classes as they are, and the literal types may come from the tests, not the declared type (F1).
  - Boxing every read typed no value (no traps): the types tsc infers from it (`new Box(x)`'s `Box<never>`, a conditional's `Box<never> | Box<string>`) still don't lower (D-195's reason), so dead regions need traps.
  - Lowering a dead unit tentatively and trapping only where it fails (to keep lies' typed uses running as the base did): D-195's reason (closures, instances and classes a unit makes; code whose types no value has lowering).
  - Reading a variable read in a nested function as unchanged (D-140's `mayHaveChanged` alone): the nested function may await while its own function assigns it (`narrowed_never_suspended_readers.pts`).
  - A dynamic result for a function declared never that returns a value (the fix commit, for an enum's `return c`): overrides, interfaces and conditionals of never functions, whose results stay void, no longer matched (F2); reverted, C239.
  - Boxing every read that may hold an untested value (round 1's fix): one that isn't stale holds a value of its declared IR type, which an index needs (round 2's F1: `indexing an array with a value`).
  - Any equality on the variable as a value test (round 1's fix): an unrelated `x === "skip"` made a dead `typeof` region live again (round 2's F4).
  - Following every const in a tested expression as a possible alias (round 2's fix): an opcode table's 300-key object literal, walked at every `code === OPCODES.OPn` for each dead read, made a dispatcher compile 1.85 times slower (review round 3); `aliasedPart` takes tsc's two alias forms only. A cache of `isDeadRead`'s answers cost more than it saved (callgrind) and was left out.
- **Intentional:** a type predicate that tests a discriminant its object's base class rewrote answers false for that class's instance (`isKnown(a): a is Add | Say` for a renamed Add): a lie about its type, so a copy of the value in its false branch throws the trap's TypeError (review round 2's F3; pinned in `narrowed_dead_branch_lies_plain.pts`), where a switch on the discriminant itself runs as Node. As D-195's instances: a lying predicate or assertion that reaches plain code tsc narrows to never by kind tests and uses the value as the narrowed type (`x + 1` as a number, `num(x)`, `new Box(x)`, a copy `const y = x`) throws the trap's TypeError, where Node runs it with the value as it is; the base rejected most of these with PTS1001 and ran some (the reproducer's matrix: 18 lies left OK, `x + 1` for a number narrowed to `number & unknown[]`, `new Box(x)`, an untyped copy that only prints). Pinned by `narrowed_dead_branch_lies_plain.pts` (no-diff).
- **Known gaps:** C237 (a stale read typed never made into a generic class in a `?:` with another type argument: `Box<never> | Box<number>`, PTS1001; H29; skipped `stale_narrowings_never_generic_boxes.pts`), C239 (a never function's `return c` returns undefined, pre-existing; H29; skipped `never_functions_return_values.pts`). Pre-existing, found on the way: C238 (an erased copy's `new Box(x).value`, H29; skipped `generic_value_erased_class_reads.pts`), C240 (a `let x: string` read before assignment, a `name!: string` field, a field an override reads during the base constructor read `""` where JS reads undefined, H29), C241 (`instanceof` with a Map, Set, function or record on the left is PTS1001, H29), C242 (`x as object | null` is PTS1001 without a reason, H22), C243 (a `this: void` parameter shifts a call's arguments, H29), C244 (`in` finds a `declare`d field, H29), C245 (a string literal cast to a mixed literal union inline as an argument, `f("left" as "up" | 3)`, is PTS1001 `converting string to number`, H29); JSON.stringify's undefined (C151, H22); a spread's copy order chosen by Go map order (C85, H22; the corpus's `transducers` C varies between builds). Still PTS1001 as on the base: an untested read whose declared IR type is a union as an array index (`null | number`), an instance's dead array index in a loop's update, and `x === SKIP` for a const literal `SKIP` (a value test, so C237's `?:` there stays PTS1001). Where tsc's never comes from tests of values, the code isn't a trap, so its typed uses read the boxed value and check it (a `?:` of `Box<never>` and `Box<string>` stays PTS1001); object types that list properties (`{v: number}`, records) are of no kind, so `Array.isArray` or `instanceof` of them is live, as in D-195.
- **Costs:** every statement of plain code is scanned for dead reads (before, instances' only), and each read tsc narrows to no value walks its flow graph (at most 4,096 nodes; past that it counts as value-tested): emit-c instruction counts +0.1% to +1.0% on ordinary programs (callgrind); one function of 150 dead `typeof` regions and 150 enum defaults +3.8%, of 285 (just under tsc's own TS2563 limit) +12% (review round 2's F5); the review's opcode dispatcher (a 300-key const table, 150 equalities, 20 exhaustive typeof chains) +7.0% (1.069G → 1.144G; +113% before aliases were restricted); the C is unchanged where nothing is narrowed to no value.
- **Upstream:** `internal/checker/pts.go` exports `PtsTypePredicateParameterIndex` and `PtsFlowTypeOfReference` (`docs/FORK.md`).
- **Tests:** `run/narrowed_dead_branches_plain.pts` and `generic_typed_never_dead_branches.pts` (unskipped; the latter's header corrected), the reproducer's `narrowed_dead_plain_forms.pts`, `narrowed_dead_plain_uses.pts`, `narrowed_dead_plain_regions.pts`, `narrowed_dead_plain_references.pts`, `narrowed_dead_plain_tail_bindings.pts`, `generic_typed_never_regions.pts`, `stale_narrowings_live_intersections.pts`, `stale_narrowings_predicate_operands.pts`, `narrowed_never_loose_locals_typed.pts` and guards `narrowed_never_enum_literal_defaults.pts`, `narrowed_never_lies_that_print.pts`, `narrowed_never_loose_locals.pts`, `narrowed_never_destinations_keep_values.pts` (its never returns moved to C239's skipped test, a guard now), `stale_narrowings_never_routes.pts`; the parent's `narrowed_never_suspended_readers.pts`, `narrowed_never_declared_type_uses.pts` (a guard); after review round 1 `narrowed_never_value_tests.pts` and `never_functions_dispatched.pts` (guards), `narrowed_dead_plain_unit_positions.pts`; after round 2 `narrowed_never_enum_indexes.pts` and `narrowed_never_discriminant_aliases.pts` (guards), `narrowed_dead_plain_literal_tests.pts`, `generic_narrowed_dead_unit_positions.pts`, `narrowed_dead_plain_booleans.pts`; after round 3 `narrowed_never_union_keys.pts`; all against Node; `narrowed_dead_branch_lies_plain.pts` (no-diff); skipped `stale_narrowings_never_generic_boxes.pts` (C237), `generic_value_erased_class_reads.pts` (C238), `never_functions_return_values.pts` (C239); `lower_test.go`'s `TestCodeThatTscNarrowsToNoValueIsATrapWhereTheNarrowingCantBeStale`; the corpus's `functional/reactive_signals.pts` (unskipped: 263 / 37).

### D-198 · The instance limit is polymorphic recursion's depth and count, not the program's own instances; past them a call takes the erased copy where nothing would be copied; no internal error after it (H09b5a); H09b5 split
- **Date:** 2026-10-21
- **Decision:** H09b5 splits into H09b5a (this: the limit and the internal error after it, C172's first two parts, C184), H09b5b (a narrower fallback, and emit-c printing the C a build compiles: C176), H09b5c (only instances calls may use: C172's growth) and H09b5d (one IR type: C177, and C172's `maxDynamicVariants`). No scope is dropped; the reproducer's 197-case matrix and its RED tests are split the same way, the later slices' committed skipped. H09b5a:
  - **The limit is polymorphic recursion's** (`instanceDepth`, `instanceLimitAt`). D-049's limit counted every instance of one generic function or class ever made, so the program's own calls side by side (70 calls of `make` for 70 classes, 65 typed calls of one generic value, 33 instances of a generic class each calling its generic method for 32 types) were PTS1001 past the 64th where Node runs them, and specialize's speculative pairings (D-190) used up a function's or class's 64 (a function with 33 real calls paired to 66; speculative class instances that a trial kept made a later real call keep the erased copy and throw). SPEC §3.7 gives the limit's purpose: a function that would need ever more instances, `f<T>` calling `f<T[]>`. Each instance's substitution now records what was being lowered or declared where it was made (`substitution.made`; `instance` marks a generic function's or class's instance, not code `erasing` extends or an erased copy; an erased copy of a generic class instance's method is made in that instance, `made: generic.outer`). An instance is past the limit where the code making it is in 64 instances of its declaration along that chain (`maxInstanceDepth`), or where polymorphic recursion has made 256 instances of its generic function declaration from the same root already (`maxRecursiveInstances`, `instanceChain`, `instanceCounts`), or 1024 of its generic function or class declaration from all roots (`maxRecursiveTotal`). An instance is made by polymorphic recursion where its chain repeats a declaration; its root is the outermost instance on its chain (one the program's code made, or a trial's for a typed call in code outside instances); a generic method's instances count together across its generic class's instances. A trial that is taken back gives back what its function instances counted against their roots' budgets (`trial.counted`), but not the totals: the class instances a failed trial made stay, and their bodies, lowered later, may start trials that make the same trees again; a class's instances count only towards its total. The counts stop recursion that branches (`f<T>` calling `f<[T]>` and `f<T[]>`) or that calls other generic functions side by side at each level within the depth, and bound what the program's many calls of a recursion and speculative pairings make together. Instances made side by side, outside any recursion, don't count against any of them, and each of the program's calls of a recursion has its own budget.
  - **Past a limit a direct call takes the erased copy where nothing would be copied** (`genericInstanceFor` returns which limit it met, unreported: `instanceLimit`; `calledInstance`, `erasedResultCopies`, `copiesFromDynamic`). A direct call (a declaration's, a method's, a static method's, a module-scope const's, an override's) 64 instances deep, or past its root's budget, calls the erased copy, its dynamic result converted to the call's type with a check (`fromErasedCall`, D-078's `fromErased`), where the generic's declared result doesn't mention its type parameters (the erased copy's result is then typed as it is) or the call's type holds no object that converting a dynamic value copies: a class's instance converts with a check, a primitive by its value, and a union, intersection, promise, iterator or iterable by what it holds. So a program that recurses a few levels runs on its instances, and one that recurses deeper runs on through the erased copy where its results are primitives (`grow`, 100 deep) and throws the checked `TypeError: Expected Box<number[]…[]>, got Box<any>` where they hold generic class instances. Where the call's type holds an array, tuple, object literal's or interface's type, map, set or function, the erased copy would make it as a dynamic one, which its typed caller would get as a checked copy, a different object where JS shares one: that call stays PTS1001, "polymorphic recursion more than 64 instances deep (`rec`) is not supported yet" (past the count: "polymorphic recursion that makes more than 256 instances (`f`) from one call is not supported yet").
