A call that looks up an instance (C184: a dispatched generic method's, D-101; an interface's or literal's by name, D-191; a generic value's or a local generic arrow's, D-190) is a demand and a trial at each level; a trial whose own instance is past a limit fails as the trial's and the call keeps the erased copy, and a limit its code meets is reported as any of its code's reports are (D-190's attribution): a direct call there takes the erased copy as above, so the trial stands, or reports, which takes the trial back. Where an instance is needed as a value (a generic function given where a non-generic function type goes, an instantiation expression, a module-scope const as a value: `genericInstance`), and for a generic class's instances, which have no erased copy, a limit is PTS1001. Past a declaration's total every case is PTS1001 ("polymorphic recursion that makes more than 1024 instances (`f`)"), which stands where a trial meets it, as the program's own, so a call doesn't keep the erased copy from its first level for want of a budget that other roots spent; where only AllInstances makes the demand, the build then falls back as the base's did (D-190; below).
  - **No type for a failed class instance** (`irType`): where a generic class's instance failed (a limit, or a superclass that isn't a class of the program, `extends Map`), irType returned the nil `*ir.Class` as a non-nil `ir.Type`, which conversions took for a class and dereferenced: PTS9001 after the honest PTS1001 (C172; `fromErased` → `dynamicConversion` → `prepareDynamicTarget`). It returns no type, so later uses don't report again.
- **Reason:** C172's acceptance for its first two parts ("only instances calls may use … count; no internal error after the limit") and C184's ("compiles (the erased copy past the limit) or an honest diagnostic, never an internal error"): the reproducer's 197-case matrix, base → this, 62 OK / 89 PTS1001 / 6 PTS9001 / 35 TYPEERR / 5 WRONG → 94 OK / 71 PTS1001 / 27 TYPEERR / 5 WRONG, no case that matched Node no longer matching it (five m8 cases, `arrowconst_70`, `decl_70`, `method_70`, `override_70`, `static_70`, go from the base's PTS1001 to the checked TypeError past the depth at run time, the known gap below); its families m1 (function limit), m2 (class leak) and m3 (the limit and its internal error) all OK; m8 (polymorphic recursion) 14 PTS1001 / 4 PTS9001 / 6 TYPEERR → 12 OK / 2 PTS1001 / 10 TYPEERR, the TypeErrors the forms that recurse 70 deep with generic class results, past 64, and the PTS1001s a recursion whose result is its type parameter (an array that deep: the base's PTS1001 too); m6 (C176's triggers) 19 → 9 TypeErrors, the rest H09b5b's. The review's probes of recursion through interfaces with `this`, local arrows, values and classes in turn, async functions, generators, `bind`, mutual, finite (40 deep) and speculative function recursion print Node's output (speculative class recursion stays PTS1001); a generic class's 33 instances × 32 types of its method compile (34 s; the base 75 s). The emit-c of the 1,143 test, corpus, docs and stress programs is byte-identical but for this slice's tests and messages and one skipped corpus program that lost two repeated errors. A depth bounds what the limit is for, and 64 keeps what D-049's count let finite recursion reach; a chain of trials costs about 80 lines of C per level of each recursive demand.
- **Alternatives rejected:**
  - The erased copy past the depth for every result (review round 1's suggestion, round 1's fix): an erased copy makes its arrays and objects as dynamic ones, which the typed caller gets as a checked copy, so a recursion whose results it kept or compared lost identity silently where the base refused to compile (review round 2's `b_identity_array`, `b_identity_object`).
  - PTS1001 for a direct call past the count (round 2's fix): the program's own calls of a linear recursion for five base types spent one budget together and stopped (review round 3's `five_chains`), which an erased copy without copies runs as Node; with side-by-side instances no longer counted, the count is reached only by recursion, which the erased copy serves as it does past the depth.
  - A limit inside a trial's code standing (the fix commit `3b2dbf4ed`, keeping D-049's PTS1001 for direct calls, the reproducer's option A): it stands only where every fallback build makes the same trial, so where only AllInstances or ValueInstances made the demand (an interface's `this.rec`, a local arrow calling a recursive declaration, a value and a declaration or a class in turn) the program built silently at a lower fallback, where the calls threw at depth 0 and unrelated calls lost their instances (review round 1's F1), and speculation's own recursion was counted as the program's (F2).
  - Every limit inside a trial the trial's, with direct calls still PTS1001 (the reproducer's P2; the first snapshot): an override calling itself directly met the limit inside the trial its dispatched caller's demand started, so that top-level call, 3 deep, lost its instance and threw where the base gave PTS1001.
  - A depth of 32 (round 1's fix, to halve the chains' cost): finite recursion 33 to 64 deep, which D-049's count allowed, went to the erased copy and threw, or was PTS1001 for a class (review round 2's `b_finite_chain_40`, `b_finite_class_chain_40`).
  - A count of every instance (1024 per declaration in round 1's fix, per genericFunc in the fix commit): per declaration it stopped a generic class's side-by-side method instances the base compiled (review round 2's 33 × 32), per genericFunc it let a branching method of a generic class make 1024 × 1024 instances (review round 1's `product_blowup`: 86 s, base 8.3 s, now 0.3 s). A count of the instances made in an instance of their own declaration, per declaration (round 2's fix): independent chains shared it (above), and instances that recursion makes of other declarations side by side, g1 → g2 → … for two type arguments each in every level of f's recursion, matched nothing (review round 3's `fanout2_9`: 26 s and 1.1 GB, now 4 s). One budget per root for all declarations (a first version): a recursion's chain and its generic class instances spent it twice as fast, so a generic function's instance calling four recursions ran out. A higher count without a depth (the reproducer's P3): polymorphic recursion's trials ran 1024 deep, 78k–116k lines of C and 31–62 s builds for programs that recurse 3 deep.
  - An erased copy's substitution without `made` (the fix commit): it cut the chain, which only the count then stopped (review round 1's `erased_chain`: 10 s, now 0.06 s).
  - Budgets per root alone (round 3's fix): giving back a failed trial's class instances' counts let them, kept and lowered later, start trials that failed in turn, a binary tree only the class depth stopped (review round 4's `giveback_classes`: still compiling after 900 s, base 0.3 s; now 1.7 s); never giving them back stops a root's later recursions where earlier failed trials spent its budget (`trial_counts`); budgets per root alone made compile time grow with the number of the program's calls (`branch_roots_40`: 40 calls, 24.7 s; now 0.3 s and the total's PTS1001, as the base); a class's per-root budget, as a function's, stopped a recursion's Box instances inside a value call's trial before its function budget sent the calls to the erased copy, so the trial was taken back and the call threw at its first level (`branch_box_via_value`).
  - Giving a failed trial's totals back (round 4's fix): each trial that a kept class instance's body started made its helper's branching tree again (review round 5's `giveback_work`: 150 s and 1.05 GB, now 1.4 s; the base 0.36 s).
  - Past a total, the trial's own failure (a first version of round 4's fix): three recursions paired with six calls spent Box's total on speculative trees, and a real call's trial, taken back, kept the erased copy and threw at its first level (`speculative_roots_3x6`), where the base reported PTS1001.
- **Supersedes:** D-049's "More than 64 instances of one function (polymorphic recursion such as `f<T>` calling `f<T[]>`) is PTS1001" (and the same count for generic classes) and SPEC §3.7's "a compile error after 64"; D-190's "the instance limit" among what a trial can't take back.
- **Known gaps:** H09b5b–H09b5d's scope. A recursion whose results hold generic class instances throws the checked TypeError past 64 levels at run time, where Node runs (the matrix's m8 `*_70`), and a branching one past its root's budget, about 8 levels of a binary tree, through dispatched demands and direct calls alike (review round 4's `branching_dispatched_deep` and round 5's `branch_box_deep`: n = 9; the latter the base's PTS1001); one whose results would be copied past the depth (arrays, object types, maps, functions, a type parameter that is one there) is PTS1001, also where the program recurses only a few levels, as on the base; so is a generic function needed past the depth as a value of a non-generic function type, and a generic class whose own members or bodies name ever deeper instances of it (`wrap(): Box<Box<T>>`, erased copies of its methods making `Box<T[]>`) (C247, H29). Branching recursion makes up to 256 instances of each function declaration per root and 1024 of each class declaration, through dispatched demands too (review round 1's `branching_dispatched`: an 8.9 s, 258 MB build; the base's PTS9001); many recursions paired speculatively with many calls stop at a total, PTS1001 where Node runs (review round 4's `speculative_roots_3x6`; the base's PTS1001 too), and so do 17 or more of the program's calls of one linear recursion (round 5's `chains_distinct_17`, as on the base), and the recursions of failed trials spend totals too; where a demand only AllInstances makes meets a total in its trial, the whole build falls back and other values lose their instances, as on the base (round 5's `total_cliff_interface`; C176, H09b5b); the demands of recursive values of one erased type pair with each other's values (two such values in one test: 16 s to build, each alone under 4 s; C172's growth, H09b5c). After the count or a failed class instance, uses of the failed type report again (`a.value`, `m.size`); where an erased copy's body fails past the depth, the error names that body, not the recursion; and at a class instance's limit its methods' parameters are reported as "using `f` from outside the function" (C246, H22). 

### D-199 · A failure only some speculative instances make leaves out only those, where no later fallback keeps them; emit-c checks its C with the build's C compiler; a link failure doesn't fall back (H09b5b)
- **Date:** 2026-10-21
- **Decision:** C176: the driver's fallback (D-190) was the whole program's, so one value whose speculative instances failed in a way a trial can't take back cost every other generic value, interface call and literal method its instances, and `pts emit-c`, which ran no C compiler, printed `AllInstances`' C where the build fell back on the C compiler's rejection.
  - **Blame** (`pts/lower/narrowing.go`). Every report is recorded with the code it was made in (`here`, a `use`): a call site whose demand is being lowered, a demand by name (`keyedInstanceCall`, D-191, D-194), a constant key's dotted call (`constantKeyCall`, C185) or a value's or dispatched method's demand with a rest parameter or a dynamic result (`siteDemandAt`, `instanceCall`); a trial, for its own code (`tryInstance`'s pairing of the demand with the erased value or generic method), whose reports it takes back as before, a call site's in it included; the rest of a statement that used what such a demand made (`usedSite`: a result that doesn't fit where it goes); or the instance being lowered. Each instance, class instance and erased copy records the code that made or found it (`substitution.users`, `ownUse`), and a method's instance its class instance (`outer`). Once the program is lowered, each report is blamed (`blameOf`) on the pairings and call sites without which none of its code would be made: through instances that make each other, the least fixed point (a class instance's method instance adds nothing to the class instance's own blame), one search per report, each substitution searched once; and for a method's instance, its class instance's where its own uses can't be left out. A report of the program's own code, or of code every fallback makes (a dispatched demand with a class's method, a declaration's value demand with a declaration's erased copy), is the program's own and stands, as before: no fallback compiled those programs, and leaving their pairings out would turn a compile error into a run-time TypeError. Each pairing and site has the last fallback that makes it (`pairing.level`: `AllInstances` for a demand by name, an adapter's or a wrapper's, D-194, and an object literal's generic method; `ValueInstances` for a generic arrow function's or function expression's erased copy, D-190, a constant key's call and a rest parameter's or dynamic result's demand; else `DeclaredInstances`, never left out). A relaxed cast (D-066) is blamed on its code or on the code that makes the objects that fail it be there (`objectBlame`: their class instance's maker, or the code that converts them to an interface on the way, `interfaceCasts`' uses), whichever a later fallback makes less of (`relaxedCastUse`). A trial is credited only with its own code (what an instance it is making declares, and the functions it made), and what a failed or foreign trial leaves pending that stays (its class instances' erased copies) is lowered outside the next trial, which would otherwise take back with itself the instances that code makes.
  - **Narrowing** (`lower.Narrowing`, `ProgramWith`'s new parameter and result; `driver.narrowedAt`). Where every diagnostic of a lowering is blamed on pairings and sites, `ProgramWith` returns the narrowing that leaves them out too; specialize skips the pairings (`specializeValue`, `specializeMethod`) and the sites lower as the first later fallback that makes no such demand does (`leavesOutSite`). The driver lowers again with it, up to 8 lowerings per fallback (`maxLowerings`), but never leaves out what a later fallback keeps: where the narrower lowering would leave out something the next fallback makes, the driver lowers the program with the later fallbacks (narrowed in turn, `laterLowering`), and unless the first that lowers leaves it out too (`Narrowing.Within`), this fallback fails and the build falls back there, as before. So no call has fewer instances than the base's fallback gave it, and only what failed, and what the next fallback wouldn't have anyway, is left out. A lowering that reports something not so blamed falls back as before.
  - **emit-c** (`driver.compileC`, `checkC`, `toolchain.CheckProgram`). EmitC and Build share one loop; emit-c checks the C of each fallback it would print with the C compiler and the release flags a build uses (`clang -fsyntax-only`, `PTS_CC` respected, the runtime's headers) and goes on where it rejects it (`rejectedC`), as a build does where the C compiler rejects its C, so it prints the C the build compiles. Without a C compiler to check with (no PTS home), it prints the C unchecked. A failure to link (an undefined reference, D-179's PTS9001) no longer falls back in a build: emit-c, which doesn't link, couldn't print the C such a build would fall back to, and it is the compiler's bug, which no case is known to hit (the reproducer's scan of 1,824 probes and the smoke test with clang found no C clang rejects).
  - **Tests:** `TestCompilerFailsCleanly` lists the seeds that compile with `AllInstances` only by narrowing (`needsNarrowing`, `driver.EmitCNarrowedWith`) apart from those that need a fallback (`needsFallback`).
- **Reason:** C176's acceptance ("only the failing values' instances are dropped; emit-c prints what the build compiles"). The reproducer's matrices, base → this: the H09b5 m6 family (25) 16 → 25 OK, its m9 family (291: 18 triggers × 7 victims × 2 placements, same-value victims, controls) 58 → 291 OK; its 12 RED run tests and the slice's 3 pass, its 6 guards of failures every level makes keep their PTS1001; the review's probes print Node's output where the base's fallback lost instances, and nothing the base printed as Node does changed.
- **Alternatives rejected:**
  - Trials that take back class instances and their bodies (lowering a trial's class instance bodies inside it): D-190 rejected rolling back every kind of state a trial touches; class instances are named by types the rest of the program shares (unions, arrays, interfaces), and a kept trial's class instance body that fails would still need the trial taken back afterwards.
  - Taking back an `AllInstances` call site's lowering tentatively where it reports (as adapters do, D-194): its conversions may make helpers and types the rest of the program keeps, and the failure may be in the use of its result, outside the call.
  - Leaving out any blamed pairing the last fallback doesn't make, at any level (the fix commit `3dde86f42`): a value pairing `ValueInstances` keeps was left out at `AllInstances` for a failure only `AllInstances`' instances caused (a relaxed cast's source, a total others spent), so the call threw where the base's fallback kept its instance (review round 1's F1). Deciding narrowability by level alone (`AllInstances` only what `ValueInstances` doesn't make, the review's prototype) keeps that, but costs every program whose failure is a value pairing's its interface calls' and literal methods' instances, the base's cliff for those (the m9 family's victims); the check against the next fallback gives both.
  - Blaming a report on the innermost trial on its chain (the fix commit): a failed trial's pending code lowered in the next trial was blamed on that trial (F2), and a class instance many trials found was blamed on the first, one pairing per lowering (F4: the cap of 8 met at 9 demands); every user of an instance is blamed, found once the program is lowered.
  - One key for a call site's demands of every kind (the fix commit): leaving out a constant key's demand by name sent its call to the value-call path, whose own failure was then taken for the same site, already left out (F3).
  - A call site's report standing in a trial, so the site is left out and the trial kept (review round 1's F7, round 1's fix): where a later fallback makes the site but not the trial, the build fell back there (round 2's R2-1); a statement's later report counted as its site's took the trial's own failures with it (R2-2); and a site left out by its node left it out of the program's own instances of the function too (R2-4). The trial takes it back, as before (C250).
  - A whole value's instances left out rather than the pairing: a value with a real instance for one demand and a failing one for another lost both (the reproducer's t12, `generic_fallback_spares_the_values_other_instances`).
  - Build not falling back where the C compiler rejects its C, so emit-c needs none: a speculative instance's C that clang rejects would stop a program that compiles without it, D-190's promise. emit-c compiling and linking too: the link depends on the optimisation level (dead code at -O2), so only a full release build answers it (the reproducer's timings: 1.3–28 s for 2k–92k lines of C, against 0.15–2 s to check).
- **Supersedes:** D-190's "a build whose lowering with `lower.AllInstances` fails … is lowered and built again with `lower.DeclaredInstances`" where the failures are blamed on pairings and sites a later fallback doesn't keep, and its fallback on C that doesn't link; D-198's "where only AllInstances makes the demand, the build then falls back as the base's did" for a total met in such a trial (now the trial's pairing is left out).
- **Known gaps:** a demand by name whose site fails inside a trial's instance (C248's `converting boolean to given`) takes the trial back, so the value keeps its erased copy where `ValueInstances` would keep its instance, as on the base (C250, H17; skipped `run/generic_fallback_site_inside_a_trial.pts`); a pairing or site is ranked by the last fallback that makes it, not capped by the code it is in, so one inside an instance only a demand by name makes is checked against `ValueInstances` and falls back with the program, as on the base (review round 2's `value_pairing_inside_by_name_trial`; C251, H09b5c); a narrowed program can make much more C than its fallback did: the trees speculative pairings made up to D-198's totals stay where they compile (review round 1's `total_cliff_interface`: 61,619 lines of C and 7.9 s against 839 lines and 0.8 s; the output is now Node's); where a total is met, the trial that meets it is blamed, not those that spent it, so a value pairing met there falls back with the program, as on the base (`generic_fallback_keeps_value_pairings_for_by_name_totals`); a failing call site's own call keeps its erased copy, whose result may throw where Node runs (C248, H17; `union_key_site`); emit-c's check adds about 0.1 s (2 s for 92k lines); and a program with an error of its own still reports the last fallback's errors after up to three lowerings, as before.

### D-200 · Narrowing distinguishes instantiated demands and owners; descriptor reuse keeps dependencies; proposed cuts are validated (H09b5b checkpoint)
- **Date:** 2026-10-21
- **Decision:** refine D-199’s implementation without changing its fallback ordering, trial rollback, erased copies or checked-any boundaries:
  - A demand/value pairing includes the demand’s instantiated checker type arguments, parameters and return type (`demandIdentity`) and the target’s enclosing generic-class parameter-to-argument bindings, including erased parameters (`substitutionIdentity`). This key is scoped to relowerings of the same checked program; checker IDs survive those lowerings, whereas IR types and substitutions are rebuilt. It is not a runtime key or a key persisted across frontend loads.
  - An existing function/method descriptor entry records every later user (`useListedInstance`, `useFrom`), even where the signature needs only one entry. A trial records its primary function substitution (`trial.root`); dependencies created by its signature and body follow their enclosing instance, so every user of the shared instance also keeps those dependencies. Reports still belong to the trial and are taken back with it.
  - `findBlame` may cache a partial result while traversing a cycle. Before a proposed substitution cut is returned by `blameOf`, validate it independently (`survives`): a substitution exists when any surviving user makes it AND its enclosing class, if any, exists. A linear forward worklist from no made nodes computes the least fixed point; an unseeded cycle cannot create itself. An unproved cut is program-owned/conservative fallback. Validation occurs before callers such as `relaxedCastUse` turn a blame into plain pairings and lose graph provenance.
- **Reason:** four source-language C176 regressions lose unrelated working instances when one source call or method declaration represents multiple instances. A separate ten-demand descriptor regression exhausts the relowering cap because the deferred dependency is blamed on only the first remaining demand. A deterministic five-node graph and a fixed-seed 10,000-graph oracle expose unsound cached outer cuts. Parent RED/GREEN and bounded checkpoint/current/Node replay are retained in [resume evidence](agent/hardening-evidence/h09b5b-resume-review.md).
- **Alternatives rejected:** source AST alone or raw resolved-signature pointers conflate different generic instances; IR pointer/keyPart identities change between relowerings; descriptor-entry deduplication without recording uses loses later demands; recording function users alone leaves signature/body dependencies credited to the first trial pairing; accepting an unvalidated cached outer alternative can omit code that still exists; validating only in `narrower` is too late for `relaxedCastUse`.
- **Scope/acceptance:** an implementation checkpoint, not completed H09b5b acceptance. The required independent review was provider content-refused and stopped; exact error and no-retry disposition are in the evidence. No replacement review is authorized by this entry. C250 → H17, C251 → H09b5c and site keys without per-instance identity remain open; the historical C251 replay probe’s improvement does not implement general containing-code level caps. No new H09b5c/H09b5d work.

### D-201 · Split source-proven function-value flow from the remaining H09b5c work
- **Date:** 2026-10-21
- **Decision:** take H09b5c1 as the next independent unblocked vertical slice under the owner's next-unblocked directive. H09b5b's independent acceptance stays blocked by the recorded provider refusal; this is neither a retry nor a substitute review. Split H09b5c without dropping scope: c1 proves source sets for ordinary function values; c2 retains method/receiver and wrapper/adapter precision, broader return/capture/container/caller flow, the remaining m4 measurements, and C251's containing-code level caps. H09b5d's checker identity and dynamic variant cap are unchanged.
- **Flow boundary:** source declarations/expressions, immutable const aliases, conditional/logical unions and comma RHS may prove the set of generic function declarations a plain value call can reach. An unassigned parameter may join all arguments of a closed, non-generic function declaration used only in direct named calls. Mutations, escaped/exported consumers, recursion/cycles, defaults/rest/pattern uncertainty, factory returns, property/container reads and other unknown sources retain the existing type-compatible pairing. Forwarders and named-method demands remain conservative in c1. A declaration reused across enclosing generic instances denotes all those instances, never a guessed per-instance origin.
- **Reason:** C172's existing skipped generated-C RED shows escaping-but-unread function values accumulating every demand's instances. A bounded source proof removes impossible pairs without weakening any/unknown conversion checks or guessing an empty source set for an unknown callee. Cache proofs and bound recursive analysis so the optimization does not introduce unbounded compiler work.
- **Review refinements:** cache a parameter's caller join at its declaration identity, shared by every read, and charge caller visits as well as uncached nodes against the 4,096-step query budget. `readSymbol` uses a shorthand property's value symbol only for its name, not for an identifier in its default initializer; that initializer can escape a consumer and must make its parameter origins unknown. Parent reproduced the missed escape as a native TypeError before this correction. Cached unknowns after depth/work exhaustion may conservatively reduce later proof precision, never denote an empty source set; that observation is not a missing-required-instance defect or a reason to broaden this slice.
- **Fixture corrections:** proven `use(make)` / `useDogs(make)` calls no longer exercise conservative speculative failures. Keep the driver's fallback assertions and the speculative-declaration error directive unchanged, but feed those guards a genuinely unknown any[] readback; retain the original declaration program as a positive run/Node control. Remove nine obsolete smoke narrowing exemptions, requiring direct AllInstances success instead. Defensive source-only function-binding writes explicitly suppress TS2630; they do not expand supported executable syntax.
- **Acceptance:** require genuine parent-executed RED→GREEN generated-C checks (both required and excluded pairs), runtime/Node unknown-flow guards, measurements, affected/full local suites, and the named independent reviewer of c1 only. The c parent stays unchecked until c2 also meets acceptance. Fresh c1 baseline at `7b3b240da`: build exit 0; four-job suite 2,122 pass / 336 skip / 0 fail, empty stderr. CI remains non-blocking per the owner directive. Final c1 local verification (2026-10-22): build exit 0, required four-job suite 2,165 pass / 335 skip / 0 fail, all ten packages PASS, corpus 263 / 37, empty stderr; parent source-hash recheck confirms all 1,548 files and CLI unchanged. The suite wrapper's gitlink-directory false additions are retained and independently corrected as bookkeeping, not hidden as a passing wrapper. Independent c1 review is ready with C252/C253 fixed, including narrow fixture follow-up. Implementation `241ba7a21` pushed to origin/main; c1 completed, c parent/c2/d remain unchecked and b acceptance blocked. See `docs/agent/hardening-evidence/h09b5c1.md`; CI pending.

### D-202 · Split returned ordinary function-value flow from the remaining H09b5c2 work
- **Date:** 2026-10-22
- **Decision:** H09b5c2a extends D-201's bounded immutable-source proof to values returned by synchronous non-generic source functions. Join all possible return expressions, not just a checker-selected branch; skip nested functions' returns. Follow only proven callable declarations/expressions and share bounded return proofs. An unknown target or return, mutation, cycle or exhausted budget retains compatible instances. Async, generator and generic factories remain unknown. No method/receiver/wrapper proof, containing-code level change, runtime conversion change or checker-identity change in this slice.
- **Reason:** c2's combined flow/containing-code scope is too large for one independently verifiable slice. Ordinary returned values have a separate source-language growth RED, required-instance guards and native/Node acceptance, without reopening the refused H09b5b review. C1's all-instance AST-source identity and nil-means-unknown convention remain authoritative.
- **Implementation boundary:** share return summaries by source function declaration identity, with no call-specific parameter environment. Parameters still join all closed direct callers. Traverse every explicit return, including unreachable/catch/finally/loop/switch arms, but skip nested callables/classes; bare returns and fallthrough add no functions. Charge body visits and every callee join, even for cached summaries, within the existing depth 128 / 4,096 steps / 256 source limits. Unknown arms and cycles are unknown unions, never empty sets. Generic exclusion uses the checker's signature parameters, including contextual generic arrows/function expressions with no syntactic type parameters; review exposed that syntactic-only screening exceeded this slice's scope. Parent source-only and direct-AllInstances contextual guards were RED before this correction and GREEN after it; this is a conservative-scope correction, not an established missing-required-instance or native-output regression. No change to existing fallback/trial behavior.
- **Remaining scope:** H09b5c2b retains method/receiver and D-194 wrapper/adapter precision, capture/container and broader caller/return flow beyond c2a, the retained m4 measurements including methods, and C251's containing-code caps. C2, c and H09b5 stay unchecked until their remaining slices meet acceptance; H09b5b's refusal remains blocked without retry or replacement.
- **Acceptance:** parent-executed RED/GREEN required/excluded direct-AllInstances checks, runtime/Node unknown-origin guards, measured code growth, named same-model isolated reproducer and independent reviewer, affected packages and a fresh required four-job full suite. CI is checked once at session start and new work is marked pending, not polled.

All 6,664 source files and final CLI unchanged during verification; no source edits or child heavy execution. Known60 returned-source growth 3,600 → 60 pairs and 44,366 → 8,907 C lines; unknown10/30 C byte-identical with all pairs retained. Evidence and safe parent/reproducer/reviewer archives in `docs/agent/hardening-evidence/h09b5c2a.md`. Implementation push/task acceptance pending at verification checkpoint; c2b scope and b's refusal unchanged.
- **Completion (2026-10-22):** implementation `5c0dce22c73c87630a93ebcc17394a48f7e23432` committed/pushed to origin/main after all local gates and corrected independent review. Accept only H09b5c2a; c2b/c2/c/b5/b remain unchecked, H09b5b's refused review untouched. CI pending, not polled; this session checkpoints and ends, next independent slice H09b5c2b.

### D-203 · Split containing-code caps from method/wrapper and broader flow (H09b5c2b)
- **Date:** 2026-10-22
- **Decision:** split H09b5c2b without dropping scope. C2b1 takes C251's containing-code level caps first: a pairing/site that exists only in AllInstances' code is not kept by ValueInstances merely because its intrinsic demand/value kind is. Determine containment from completed use information, including descriptor reuses and enclosing generic classes, not the first speculative maker. Unknown or shared surviving origins remain conservative. C2b2 retains method/receiver, wrapper/adapter and broader source-flow precision, capture/container/caller/return work beyond D-202, and m4 method-growth measurements.

The owner authorizes C251 under the first unblocked c scope. H09b5b's refused independent-review gate stays blocked: reproducer/reviewer work in c2b1 is only for these new caps and their regressions, never replacement acceptance of b.
- **Acceptance:** fresh parent baseline; independently designed and parent-executed current RED before implementation; graph and native/Node required-instance guards including shared own/ValueInstances contexts; scoped same-runtime independent review; affected tests, clang smoke and required four-job full suite. Retain real outputs, including historical probes already green. Commit/push locally green work, mark CI pending and end after this slice. No change to fallback order, trial rollback, checked-any boundaries, source-flow proofs or checker-instance identity.

### D-204 · Containing-code caps use completed positive availability without changing pairing identity (H09b5c2b1)
- **Date:** 2026-10-22
- **Decision:** retain intrinsic demand/value/site levels in stable narrowing keys. Record every occurrence's actual demand-code and value-code owners, including omitted-site checks, separately. After lowering finishes, a bounded positive graph computes each omitted pairing's effective last fallback: occurrences join by OR and require both owners; substitutions join all completed users and require their enclosing class. Program-owned and unknown sources remain live; unrecorded pairings retain intrinsic availability. Worklist propagation starts without speculative code, so unseeded cycles cannot invent instances and later own/ValueInstances users keep shared code alive. Both occurrence storage and graph nodes plus rules are bounded at 100,000; exhaustion discards the cap proof, not any instance. Report-only statement-site provenance is marked and conservatively live in availability, without changing report blame.
- **Reason:** an intrinsic ValueInstances pairing inside AllInstances-only code is absent at ValueInstances. First-maker provenance, early caps and statement-level report heuristics do not prove that absence; all contexts, descriptor hits and class dependencies must be considered. Raw keys must still match across relowerings and across generic instances sharing an AST site.
- **Alternatives rejected:** cap the key during pairing construction (later users can raise availability); recursively reuse partial blame cuts (cycles and enclosing-class alternatives require global validation); treat the rest of a statement as contained in its call site (it is only a report dependency); guess a cap when a budget expires (unsafe).
- **Scope:** C251 only, under D-203. No change to source-flow pruning, fallback order, trial rollback, checker identity, checked-any boundaries, or H09b5b's blocked independent acceptance. Implementation verification is recorded in `docs/agent/hardening-evidence/h09b5c2b1.md`; no broader method/wrapper-flow completion is inferred.

### D-205 · Split exact wrapper provenance from broader receiver/source flow (H09b5c2b2)
- **Date:** 2026-10-22
- **Decision:** H09b5c2b2a takes exact creation-site provenance for method-value and zero-bound-argument bind wrappers, and carries captured-expression sources through D-194 value forwarders and non-generic instantiating adapters. Reuse the existing bounded source sets, closed named-parameter joins and ordinary return summaries. Do not infer runtime receiver identity from a static type. Generic-context wrapper creation sites and unknown/any/mutable sources remain conservative. H09b5c2b2b retains broader receiver dispatch, capture/container and caller/return precision beyond these proofs, with no dropped scope.
- **Reason:** exact wrapper creation and captured-value forwarding have their own generated-C growth RED and required-instance/native guards. The retained m4 methods family passes distinct method wrappers to distinct consumers, so it measures this boundary without general receiver analysis. Combining all remaining flow forms would make one independently testable slice too broad.
- **Acceptance:** parent-executed required/excluded AllInstances RED→GREEN, Node/native uncertainty and receiver/capture guards, retained method-growth measurements, named same-runtime isolated reproducer and scoped independent reviewer, affected packages, clang smoke and a fresh required four-job full suite. The AST source identity stands for every closure made at that site, never a particular receiver or capture. Existing type compatibility, fallback order, trial rollback and checked-any boundaries are unchanged. No claim that the compatible-candidate enumeration itself is eliminated.
- **Boundary:** only this new c2b2a scope is authorized for its agents; H09b5b's refused review remains blocked and is not retried, rephrased or replaced. C2b2b and all parents remain unchecked. CI checked once at session start; no failed run among the latest ten, pre-implementation planning `8023eb66e` passed run 39643601236, c2a acceptance and c2b1 implementation runs were cancelled/superseded, c2b1 acceptance was queued. Receipts preserve those exact roles; new work is CI pending, never polled.
- **Implementation:** register opaque wrapper sites only at actual `addForwarder`, outside erased/generic lexical contexts (checker-contextual functions included); bind tokens name full calls. Unregistered/cached-unknown sources remain unknown, never empty. Captured-value demands carry the captured expression, not the later caller's source. Depth/work/source-count bounds and conservative named receiver dispatch remain unchanged.
- **New pre-existing issue:** C254 → H29: an inline `held[0] as Factory` expression in a non-generic function context keeps `Box<any>` and throws where an explicit checked `Factory` binding adapts and Node succeeds. Parent independently verified ordinary-arrow/bind variants with exact-base/current AllInstances and default builds; no regression and no intentional-semantics ruling.
