### D-216 · Instances for type arguments of one IR type are told apart where the types they compute differ: functions by those types' keyParts, every typed demand keyed; a build splits generic classes only where it doesn't lower with them shared; no dynamic-variant cap (H09b5d)
- **Date:** 2026-10-23
- **Decision:** H09b5d (C177, and C172's last part) is one slice, no split; three review rounds.
  - **Functions** (`genericInstanceFor`, `computedTypes`, `computesFromTypeParameters`, `typeArgumentsIdentity`): instances were keyed by their type arguments' IR types (D-049) and literal type arguments (D-089), and a typed call reused the first instance of those where its checker types gave the instance another signature or body (`pick<Promise<number>>` and `pick<Promise<string>>`, both dynamic values, whose `Box<Awaited<T>>` is a Box<number> and a Box<string>: PTS1001 `using a Box as a Box`; `{ kind: "a" }` and `{ kind: "b" }`, one shape; a branded Id and its number; a body that parses into a `K<T>`), and a dynamic-result call made up to four such instances (`maxDynamicVariants`, D-190's `madeFor`), past which it kept the erased copy and threw. Now a declaration's computed types are the types in its signature, its body's expressions and type nodes and its calls' type arguments that tsc computes from its type parameters (`computesTypes`: a conditional type, an indexed access, `keyof`, a mapped or template literal type, also inside a program interface's members, mentioning them; at most 64, past which its type arguments themselves stand in). Where it has none, its types are made of its type arguments' IR types, and instances stay keyed by IR types and literal type arguments. Where it has some, instances are also told apart by those types for the call's type arguments (`instanceSubstitution`), written by keyParts with brands, literals as their primitives and no IR type made (`keyIdentity.typeArguments`, `widenLiterals`: a program interface by its members, object types by members with methods marked, past six levels or 256 parts one part, recursion through `Promise<T>` meeting a part made before): `Box<Awaited<T>>` tells Promise<number> from Promise<string> and from any, `A["type"]` doesn't tell 200 action types apart (each a string). `madeFor` and `maxDynamicVariants` go.
  - **Brands** (`typeArgument`, `substituted`, `normalized`): an instance's type arguments keep their brands (`Id = number & { __brand: "id" }`), which instantiate dropped (C221), so `T extends Id ? string : number` is the call's; every IR type made of them is still the primitive's.
  - **Demands** (`demandAt`, `instantiatingAdapterSkipping`, `instanceKey`): every typed demand has its key, so calls through generic values, dispatched, interface and literal methods, local arrows, adapters and forwarders whose signatures are the same IR types find the instance of their own checker types (the reproducer's body-only cases: the value's function type computes nothing, its body does). A typed result's key is its type arguments', parameters' and result's keyParts with brands; a dynamic result's also names its function type's declaration (D-190's, unchanged).
  - **Classes** (`lower.ProgramSplittingClasses`, the driver's `compileC`; `sharesClass`, `classMembersIdentity`, `classComputedTypes`, `memberPart`, `classesIn`): a generic class's instance is a nominal IR class the program converts between where TypeScript does, and splitting instances of one IR type breaks that: TS-legal conversions (`Task<Promise<number>>` to `Task<unknown>` both before and after `Task<Promise<string>>` is made), erased copies' `Task<any>`, factories and classes that name the class (`make<T>(): Task<T>`, `Outer<T> { inner: Task<T> }`), inheritance chains (review rounds 1 and 2). So a build lowers a program with each generic class's instances of one IR type shared, as before, and only where no lowering succeeds that way (every fallback, D-190, D-199) lowers it again splitting them, keeping the first failure if that fails too. Split, instances of one IR type are two classes where the class computes from its type parameters a superclass that differs (`Sub2<T> extends Base2<Awaited<T>>`), or a member type (a property's, method's, constructor parameter's) whose part differs and either neither instance's converts to the other's (members one by one: tsc's variance takes Holder<1> for a Holder<number>) or they name other classes (two classes of one shape); and a reference to such a class counts as computing for functions.
  - **Narrowings of dynamic type arguments** (`narrowedInstance`): where a type argument whose IR type is dynamic narrows to no value (`Promise<number> & any[]` after `Array.isArray(x)`) in code D-195 keeps live (it judges deadness by unknown, the widest type of that IR type), the narrowing is unknown's (`any[]`, `Box<any>`), not never, whose members don't lower (`x.length`: PTS1001 on the base where that instance was the first or only one). A class-typed argument (`Box<Json>`) keeps never.
  - **Variables of dynamic type arguments** (`storedDynamic`): in an instance, a variable whose declared type's instantiation has a dynamic IR type (`x: T` for unknown or a Promise) is stored dynamic, as an unknown one in plain code is, so its narrowed members are a dynamic value's (C265: `x instanceof Map` then `x.size` was PTS9001 for unknown).
- **Reason:** C177's acceptance (calls whose type arguments of one IR type give other results run, or an honest diagnostic) and C172(d)'s. The reproducer found the sharing in instances' bodies through every call form (its m10 matrix: 213 of 291 TypeErrors on the base), so IR signatures alone can't key instances. Review round 1 found the first fix's costs (every function split by type arguments: 615 → 27,281 lines for a 200-action dispatcher; recursion through promises to the depth limit; phantom class instances) and regressions (class splits stopping TS-legal conversions); round 2 that splitting classes at all breaks programs the base ran (dependents, order, erased copies), which no rule on classes alone avoids, since their instances are nominal; so splitting is kept for programs that fail without it.
- **Alternatives rejected:**
  - Every function instance keyed by its type arguments' keyParts (the first fix, `ebf0f8bb1`; the reproducer's prototype A), and computing functions keyed so (`011c7f6a8`): round 1's F3–F5 and F7, round 2's F5 (`A["type"]` for 200 actions: 27,281 lines, a 61 s build).
  - Keying by each instance's IR signature (the reproducer's prototype B): the body-only cases stay TypeErrors.
  - Splitting classes in every build, by computed members (`ebf0f8bb1`), by whole-type assignability (a first version of round 2: tsc's variance takes Holder<1> for a Holder<number>), or by members one by one (`011c7f6a8`): round 1's F1–F2 and round 2's F1–F3, conversions TypeScript allows PTS1001 or a TypeError where the base ran.
  - Erasing computed member types in shared classes (dynamic members): bodies that make `new Box(v.value)` from them make a Box<any>, which doesn't convert to the call's Box<string> (D-078).
  - A typed demand's key naming its function type's declaration (`011c7f6a8`): ten parameters of separately declared `<T>(x: T) => Box<T>` types made ten entries for one instance (851 → 1,089 lines); the instance depends on the instantiated types alone (review round 2 found no counterexample).
  - Coarser keys for typed demands (literals widened): the body-only twins through a value whose type computes nothing need the precise key.
  - Unknown's narrowing for every argument whose widest type is unknown: `Box<Json>`'s instance read a Box<undefined>, another class.
- **Costs:** of the 1,513 test, corpus, docs and stress programs the base compiles or rejects, emit-c (base `fd90928f0` vs `fb68242c6`) is byte-identical for 1,487; four RED tests that were PTS1001 compile; the other 22 grow 34,773 → 35,184 lines of C (+1.2%), from keyed typed demands' entries and the instances computing functions make per computed types (`church_combinators.pts` 5,515 → 5,647; `generic_values_nongeneric_contexts.pts` 3,092 → 3,158). The review's growth probes: a 200-action dispatcher 615 lines as on the base, also with an `A["type"]` result (27,281 in `011c7f6a8`); calls through generic values for many literal types of one IR type grow (C266: 853 → 7,221 lines for five values and 200 types). A program that doesn't lower with classes shared is lowered a second time (t02, both failing: 0.16–0.18 s → 0.23–0.29 s). The reproducer's m5 family 4 → 9 OK of 9 and m7's clean pairs 12 of 12 in every form (the `any`/`unknown` pairs stay PTS1001: that call fails alone, D-078).
- **Known gaps:** classes keep D-050's nominal instances: a class that computes types only in its methods' bodies (C260), instances whose computed members convert one way, which share the first's class (C261), three instances where the third converts to two that can't share (C262), members and factories that name a split class or a generic interface computing from T, and inheritance chains, when split (C263). Functions: a nested direct call whose callee alone computes (C267), a type computed only in an overload's signature (C268). Typed demands keyed by literal-precise keys add an entry per key and value, and a call through a value finds its entry by a linear scan (C266: 200 action types through five values, 853 → 7,221 lines, and a hot call 4–6× slower; deferred to H27, DEFERRED.md). Review round 3: a class conflict inside a speculative instance (a generic value's, an interface's or an abstract method's) takes its trial back, so no lowering fails and the build doesn't split (C269); a conflict only run time shows keeps the shared class (C270); splitting is the whole program's, so where one class needs it an unrelated erased copy's `Task<any>` can be only one split class (C271; the base's PTS1001 becomes a run-time TypeError); a computed literal widened before it reaches a callee's literal type argument (C267). Pre-existing: a dispatched or interface generic method returning `Box<Awaited<U>>` throws for one type argument (C264); D-195's deadTest widens a type argument to unknown, for which a conditional type it computes may be another branch, so live code is a trap (C272, high).
- **Tests:** unskipped `run/generic_instances_same_ir_type_{promises,twins}.pts`, `generic_class_instances_literal_type_arguments.pts`, `generic_instances_branded_type_arguments.pts`, `generic_values_same_ir_type_promises.pts`, `generic_dynamic_variants_past_cap.pts`; new `generic_narrowed_instances_{wider_first,dynamic_arguments}.pts`, `generic_instanceof_map_unknown.pts`; the reproducer's 63 (C260–C262 skipped); 49 from the review rounds' probes (guards for every regression a round found, base failures now fixed, C263, C264, C267–C272 skipped); `lower_test.go`'s `TestInstancesForTypeArgumentsOfOneIRTypeAreToldApartWhereTheirTypesDiffer`, `TestClassInstancesOfOneIRTypeAreSplitOnlyWhereTheirComputedTypesCantShare`; six IR goldens print typed instance calls' keys.
- **Supersedes:** D-049's and D-050's "one instance per list of IR types" where a declaration computes types from its type parameters (functions) or a program doesn't lower with a class's instances shared (classes); D-089's literal-type-argument split stays; D-190's `maxDynamicVariants` and `madeFor`, and its key for dynamic results only.

### D-217 · `make test` sets a 40-minute test timeout; CI's PTS job gets 60 minutes
- **Date:** 2026-10-23
- **Decision:** CI runs `PTS_REQUIRE_NODE=1 make test`, which ran `go test` with its default 10-minute per-binary timeout. `pts/tests` took 544 s on CI's runner at `ebf0f8bb1` (run 39942688251, green) and passed 600 s once H09b5d's review rounds added tests: runs 39953639315 (`011c7f6a8`) and 39960481931 (`fb68242c6`) failed with `panic: test timed out after 10m0s` while corpus and number-corpus tests were still running normally (no test hung; every goroutine was in `waitParallel` or a running comparison). `make test` now passes `-timeout 40m`, the timeout PROGRESS.md already gave for local full suites, and the PTS tests job's `timeout-minutes` goes from 45 to 60 so a slow run fails on the test timeout with its goroutine dump rather than on the job limit.
- **Alternatives rejected:** sharding `pts/tests` across jobs (more CI wiring for a suite that runs in about 11 minutes); skipping tests on CI (hides coverage).

### D-218 · `x instanceof C` for a generic class: the reference is the instance of C its declared type implies, or else a dynamic value; H10 split into H10a/H10b (H10a)
- **Date:** 2026-10-24
- **Decision:** H10 is split, once (owner's rules, 2026-10-22): **H10a**, references narrowed by `instanceof` to a generic class and their uses where the narrowing holds; **H10b**, values narrowed so that flow out of the narrowing (C279). tsc narrows `x instanceof C`, for a generic class C, to C's prototype's type `C<any, …>` unless x's declared type is C or derives from it (`getNarrowedType` with checkDerived): from an interface C implements, a generic or plain base class, `object`, `{}`, unions of these, a type parameter (`S & C<any, …>`). PTS makes a class per instance (D-050), so `C<any, …>` is a class of its own that x's object isn't: D-149's checked downcast threw (`Expected MapLRU<any, any>, got MapLRU<string, number>`), a generic base class was PTS1001 (`using a Option as a Some`), a union PTS1001 (C10).
  - **The instance the declared type implies** (`instanceofNarrowing`, `PtsInferInstanceTypeArguments` in `internal/checker/pts.go`): a variable, parameter, property or element at a literal key (`arr[0]`) that tsc types `C<any, …>` (or `S & C<any, …>`) where its declared type is neither that nor a union holding it is C's instance for the type arguments the declared type implies, where it implies one instance alone (review round 1's R1-F8): a class's place holds the instances of the subclasses whose superclass is that class instance, so a class source decides a type parameter that C's `extends` chain passes up as it is (`Option<number>` for `Some<T> extends Option<T>`; not `Weird<A> extends Bag<A | string>`, which several A give); an interface's place holds the objects that provide its members with the same types, so an interface or object type decides those its members mention, as tsc infers them structurally (`Cache<string, number>` to `MapLRU<K, V>`'s `get(key: K): V | undefined`; not a phantom parameter, `Tagged<T> implements Named<T>` with no T in Named), and an argument C's `implements` clause passes up as it is must agree. A union's members decide alone and must agree, and one that implies no instance but can hold any of C (`object`, `{}`) leaves it undecided (R1-F7). The instance must also be assignable to the declared type. Results are cached per reference, tsc's type and declared type, so each instance of a generic function or class has its own (R1-F1). `typeAt` and `receiverClass` give it for the reference, so its members are that instance's, and `narrowed` converts the reference to it with D-149's checked downcast, also from a union (`Cache<string, number> | undefined`) or a type parameter's type argument.
  - **Members tsc types with any** (`isInstanceofDynamicMember`, `instanceofMember`): a member read or method call on such a reference whose type in tsc mentions `any` (C<any, …>'s member for C's type parameters: `c.val`, `c.items` an `any[]`, `c.pair` an `[any, any]`, `c.next` a C<any, …>, a callback field), where the instance's is typed, is the instance's own value held as a dynamic value: what uses it takes it as tsc says, operators as on `any` (`c.key + c.val`), its own members read, written and called at run time (`c.items.push(x)` reaches the instance's array, `c.pair[1]` its tuple's element; R1-F4), and a typed place that takes a dynamic value with a check (`any`, a primitive, a class) converts it so; so the narrowed code compiles wherever tsc's types go, as before H10, which code that never runs needs (R1-F3). `mentionsAny` also looks into index signatures and mapped types (`Record<string, V>`, R2-F2) and is memoized (R2-F6's compile time).
  - **No copies that writes would miss** (review round 2's R2-F1): a variable without a type annotation, or a `for…of` binding, whose value is such a dynamic value (`holdsInstanceofDynamicValue`: a member tsc types with any, an uninferred narrowing or a member of one, what their members, elements and calls give) and whose type a dynamic value would convert to by a copy (dynamic data, an object type) or is C<any, …> (`let n = c.next`) holds it as a dynamic value (`isInstanceofDynamicLocal`), as a variable declared `unknown` does, so `const it = c.items; it.push(2)` reaches the instance's array, for `unknown` sources too (D-179's copy for them is superseded for such locals). Where such a member goes into another place a dynamic value would be copied into (an annotated variable, a return, a parameter, an assignment, a literal's element: `copiesDynamicValue`), it stays the instance's typed value, which converts as typed values do (D-094): a copy where nothing can see it change (a new array, a parameter only read), and otherwise PTS1001 `using a number[] as a value[] where it may be changed or kept`, not a silent copy. A read of a dynamic value's member that tsc types C<any, …>, including private fields and `?.` chains on it, stays the dynamic value. Review round 3 found 14 more places a copy still happened (a conditional, `??`, `||`, `as`, a default parameter, `||=`, `yield`, for…of destructuring, a dynamic local passed on), so the rule holds at the conversion itself (`copiesInstanceofDynamicValue`, in `convert` and `??`'s lowering): converting a dynamic value that an instanceof narrowing gives (`holdsInstanceofDynamicValue`, also through conditionals, `??`, `||`, `&&`, `as`, `satisfies`, `await`, assignments) into a type that holds dynamic values (`any[]`, an object type with an `any[]` property), which an instance's typed member reaches only as a copy, is D-094's PTS1001, except for a parameter that only reads it and a `.par` view whose method only reads; a target of the instance's own types (`number[]`, `{ items: number[] }`) keeps the object. Names a pattern binds from such a value (`for (const { list } of c.groups)`) and variables with conditional or `satisfies` initializers are dynamic locals too, and a dynamic value of an intersection with C<any, …> (`S & Box<any>` in an instance) is a narrowed dynamic value. The dynamic-local answers are per instance of the code being lowered (R3-F1, the class of R1-F1). Review round 4: the guard covers only values H10a made dynamic (`madeDynamicByInstanceof`), not the members of a variable declared `unknown` or `any` or of a `catch` binding, which were dynamic values before and copy into typed places as D-179 says, so programs that only read them compile as before (R4-F1); a parameter without a type of a callback that a method of such a value is given (`c.items.forEach((x, i, arr) => …)`) holds what it is given as a dynamic value, and one annotated with a type holding dynamic values (`(row: any[]) => …`) is PTS1001; a destructuring default makes a dynamic local too; object literals' shorthand and explicit properties (`{ it }`), `Promise.resolve(…)` and `await` of such a value are seen through (R4-F2). Review round 5: a destructured callback parameter (`forEach(({ list }) => …)`) is a dynamic value too; an annotated callback parameter is PTS1001 only where an array method hands it the receiver's objects (an element, `arr`; not reduce's accumulator) and it isn't only read (R5-F1); a read-only `.par` view may take a copy only of an array of primitives in the inferred instance, since a copy of arrays or objects copies them too and element writes would miss (R5-F3). A call of a function-valued field, and of a method with a function, object or array literal argument for a parameter whose type in tsc mentions `any` (`c.update(k, (o) => ({ a: o.a + 1 }))`), is a call through the member found at run time, which converts the arguments with checks. Session 6, after review round 5 (R5-F2's rest and what the parent found with it, C283, C284): **callbacks are judged where they are given** (`copiesCallbackArgument`, in `dynamicCallWith`): a function given to a method called through `any` of a dynamic value an instanceof narrowing made (`c.items.forEach(f)`, `o.each(f)` for an uninferred `o`, `c.inner.each(f)`, `c.store.forEach(f)`, the narrowed instance's own method called with a literal) with a parameter of a type a dynamic value converts to by a copy (`arr: any[]`, `copiesInstanceObjects`) that the method may hand the instance's objects is PTS1001 at the argument where it is given by reference (a function declaration, a const's function, an object's method, a function value a parameter holds) or inside an object or array literal argument (a value of the literal's type, `{ cb: addOne }`, `[addOne]`), unless its code is known (written there, a function declaration, a const's function) and only reads that parameter (D-094). An array's method hands its callback (its first argument) the element and the array, and reduce's accumulator only where no initial value is given, which is then the first element (`arrayCallbackObjectParameters`); any other method may hand any parameter anything. A callback written as the argument holds what it is given at such a parameter as a dynamic value, annotated or not (`handsInstanceObjects`): `(row: any[]) => row.push(1)`, which round 4 made PTS1001, now runs as Node (tie-breakers 2 and 3: what Node does, where it can be done, over a compile error), except reduce's own accumulator and parameters of the instance's own types (`(row: number[]) => …`), which keep their types; and its result, where it declares no type, goes back to the method as a dynamic value (`returnsToDynamicInstanceCall`: `return acc`, `c.update(k, (old: any[]) => { old.push(1); return old; })`). **Objects inside such a dynamic value** (`isInstanceofNestedObject`): an element, property or method result of one (`c.matrix[0]`, `c.meta.tags`, `c.pairs[0][1]`, `c.store.get(k)!`, `c.matrix.at(-1)!`, `c.slots[0]?.push(x)`, `c.maybe?.tags`) of a type a copy would have is the dynamic value it is, in `isNarrowedDynamic`, `narrowedValue`, `narrowedMember` and `?.` chains, for `unknown` and uninferred sources too: `narrowedValue` converted it to tsc's `any[]` by a copy, so `c.matrix[0].push(5)`, `const r = c.matrix[1]; r.push(6)` and `c.meta.tags.push(1)` were lost silently and `c.matrix[0] === c.matrix[0]` was false, and calls on method results (`c.store.get(k)!.push(2)`) were PTS1001 (C284). Review round 6 and session 6's `reproducer` (NOT READY, R6-F1–F7, N1–N15) on that commit: the nested-object test asked each object of a member chain twice, so compile time doubled with each level in every program with a generic class (R6-F5, N1: 26 s at depth 22); `isNarrowedDynamic`'s and `instanceofDynamicSource`'s answers are kept while one question is answered (`narrowingAnswers`) and `isInstanceofNestedObject` asks only of types that mention any. A read-only parameter (`isReadOnlyArgument`, a by-reference callback's known code) or a new array (D-094) takes a copy only where the copy's elements aren't copies too (`copiesElements`, `copiesInstanceElements` in D-094's typed path): `firstOf(c.matrix).push(2)`, `forEachOf(c.matrix, …)` and `c.matrix.forEach(addToFirst)` with `all: any[][]` read only the outer array and wrote to a copied row (R6-F1); a by-reference function that hands its array parameter to a callback declaring the array (`row.forEach((x, i, a) => { a[i] = 8; })`) doesn't only read it (`handsToCallback`). Reduce's accumulator counts as handed objects unless the initial value is a new array (R6-F2: `c.items.reduce(collect, c.tags)`, `…, c.matrix[1])`). Spread arguments are judged as the arguments they become (`copiesSpreadCallbacks`, R6-F3's `c.matrix.forEach(...args)`). Rest parameters of inline callbacks keep their own new arrays (N2: an annotated `(...args: any[])` was PTS9001), and annotated parameters whose type is a dynamic value (`(row: unknown)`) are dynamic locals too. `x as T` of such a value whose type would copy keeps the object (N3: `(arr as any[]).push(x)`, `c.items as unknown as any[]`, `const a = c.items as any[]; a.push(1)`, which round 3 made PTS1001: `errors/generic_instanceof_sink_as_any.pts` is now `run/generic_instanceof_casts_and_callbacks.pts`'s first line). A new array made from such a value (`c.items.filter(…)`, `slice`) converts by a copy where its elements aren't copies too, and `.par` views take copies of nested arrays and new arrays whose instance type holds primitives (`instanceTypeOf`, `instanceResultType`; R6-F4, N4). `concat` takes an array held as a dynamic value as an array to spread, copied with checks (N5). Callbacks of methods of `unknown` values narrowed to the class (`u.each((rows) => …)`) hold what they are given too (N15). A function given by reference to a method that keeps it (`c.handlers.set(k, f)`, a user method storing it) stays PTS1001: whoever calls it later may hand it the instance's objects, which it would get as copies (R6-F6; `errors/generic_instanceof_callback_kept_handler.pts`, also for an instance for any, whose objects are dynamic data already), and the message names the function's parameter (`giving a function whose parameter is a value[] to a method of the object an instanceof narrowing holds as a dynamic value, …`). Review round 7 (NOT READY, R7-F1–F3) on that commit: reduce's accumulator counted unless the initial value was a new array, so by-reference reducers given `{}`, `new Map()` or a local of the accumulator's own type were PTS1001 (R7-F1); the accumulator is the callback's own where the initial value is a new array, object or collection or a value of the accumulator's type that no instanceof narrowing gives (`ownsAccumulator`), and otherwise (`c.tags`, a `number[]` for an `any[]` accumulator) is handed objects. Functions an instanceof narrowing's dynamic value keeps were judged only as call arguments (R7-F2): an array member's `push`, `unshift`, `splice` and `fill` keep them (`keepingArrayMethods`: every parameter, as another method's), and an assignment of a function, or of a value holding one, into such a value (`c.list[0] = f`, `c.cfg.onRows = f`, `c.list = [f]`, `c.list = c.list.concat(…)`) is PTS1001 where it has a copying parameter (`storesCopyingFunction`, `storing a function whose parameter is a value[][] in the object an instanceof narrowing holds as a dynamic value, …`); an annotated inline callback given to such a keeping method holds what it is later given. A by-reference function that hands its array to a named callback reads it only if that callback's known code declares fewer parameters than the array's position (R7-F3). Review round 8 (NOT READY, R8-F1–F3) on that commit: a function read through a narrowed member into a local was an adapter, so `const h = s.listeners[0]; s.listeners.indexOf(h)` was -1 and a listener was never removed (R8-F1): a local without a type bound to such a function holds it as it is (dynamic locals include function types), and calls of it (`h(x)`, `h.bind(null)(x)`) and its `call`, `apply` and `bind` are calls through `any`, whose results convert to tsc's types as narrowed reads do; storing the instance's own functions back (`c.cfg.onRows = c.list[0]`, `c.list = c.list.toReversed()`) is not reported, as they need no adapter (R8-F2); destructuring assignments and `Object.assign` into such a value are judged as the assignments they do (R8-F3, `isInstanceofDynamicPlace`). Review round 9 (NOT READY, R9-F1–F3) on that commit: "holds such a value" was true when any branch of a conditional, `??` or `||` or a `concat` on a member did, so `c.cfg.onRows = c.maybe ?? addRow`, a local `flag ? c.list[0] : addRow` given to `push` and `c.list = c.list.concat([addRow])` lost writes silently (R9-F1); a value counts as the instance's own only where every part is (`isInstanceOwnValue`: both branches, every element and property of a literal that holds a copying function, `concat`/`with`/`toSpliced` arguments), function-typed dynamic locals only for such values, and a dynamic argument that may be the program's function is judged by tsc's type; the instance's own functions stored back through literals are not reported (R9-F2); a call of a callee holding such a value gives a value an instanceof narrowing holds, so its results stay the instance's objects (R9-F3: `const mk = c.mkList; mk().push(2)`). Review round 10 (NOT READY, R10-F1–F2) on that commit: reads of a `concat` or `with` result holding the program's function (`c.list.concat([addRow])[1]`, `.at(-1)`, `.find(…)`, a `forEach` parameter of it) counted as the instance's own and lost writes (R10-F1); `concat`, `with` and `toSpliced` keep their arguments as `push` does (`keepingArrayMethods`), so the function is judged where it is given. `??`, `||` and `&&` between the instance's own functions stay the functions they are (`isInstanceOwnFunction` in `logical` and `nullishCoalescing`), where they were adapters and `===` and `indexOf` missed them (R10-F2). Review round 11 (NOT READY, R11-F1–F2, both Low): `??` between two optional members holding the instance's functions, whose result tsc types `fn | undefined`, was still an adapter (R11-F1: the own-function test takes the non-undefined part); an array literal of the instance's own functions given to `concat`, now a keeping method, was PTS1001 (R11-F2: arguments wholly the instance's own are skipped, also `c.boxes.push({ cb: c.list[0] })`).
  - **Where tsc's C<any, …> goes** (`classCast`): the reference converted to another instance of its class or a subclass of one (`const m = c`, an inferred return; H10b's C279) is a checked conversion where it runs, as the base's downcast was, not a PTS1001; one to an unrelated class tsc takes structurally stays PTS1001 (R2-F4). A private method is never called dynamically (R2-F3).
  - **A dynamic value where the declared type implies no instance** (`isUninferredInstanceof`): a plain base class, `Error`, `object`, `{}` (also as a type parameter's `S & C<any, …>`, R1-F9), an interface that doesn't mention every type parameter, a union whose members imply different instances or hold any instance, a property or element declared `unknown` or `any`: the reference is what it holds, as a dynamic value, whose members are found at run time, as an `unknown` variable's narrowings are (D-179). A write or a pass to a typed place that the object's instance doesn't hold is D-070/D-072's checked TypeError. The object of a private member written or called there (`o.#v = x`, `o.#tick()`) keeps the base's checked downcast to tsc's C<any, …>, which an instance for any passes (R1-F2). Declared types are found for private names (`this.#cache`), properties found through an index signature (`rec.main`) and imported bindings (R1-F10).
  - **Members of instances held as dynamic values** (`isMemberOfNarrowedInstance`): a member that tsc types as dynamic data (`any[]`) of a dynamic value narrowed to a generic class stays that value (isNarrowedDynamic, narrowedValue), so calls and element writes through it reach the instance's own typed array; `x.items.push(8)` and `x.items[0] = 70` changed a copy (C273, also for `unknown` sources before H10). Bound to a variable without a type annotation it stays that value too (round 2, below; `run/generic_instanceof_dynamic_member_locals.pts`, where D-179 copied it).
  - **Private fields of instances held as dynamic values** (`ir.FamilyField`, cgen's `familyField`): `o.#f` reads the field of whichever of C's instances o is, tested in turn where the C is written (when the instance list is complete), and throws V8's `TypeError: Cannot read private member #f from an object whose class did not declare it` for another object; it looked up a property named `#f`: undefined (C274), also where any value goes (`Number(o.#v)`, templates, R1-F6). Private accessors and methods are found by name in method tables, as before (R1-F5).
  - **An instance made inside a method of its own class** (`ensureMembers`, `memberParameterLocals`): declaring the new instance's members rebound the parameter symbols the method being lowered uses (`new Box<boolean>(…)` or `o instanceof Box` in `Box<number>`'s method: PTS1001 `using \`o\` from outside the function`, C276); they are restored, as `declareInstance` restores a generic function's.
- **Reason:** C10 (GPT #3, Grok #3). Accepted 2026-10-24 after review round 12 (READY): rounds 6–11 of session 6 (R6-F1–F7, R7-F1–F3, R8-F1–F3, R9-F1–F3, R10-F1–F2, R11-F1–F2) each fixed or recorded; the 777-case matrix 230 → 622 OK across all rounds, none worse, none wrong. Review round 6 (NOT READY) and session 6's reproducer on the first session-6 commit found its exponential compile time (R6-F5, N1), deep copies through read-only exemptions (R6-F1, N8), reduce's instance initial values (R6-F2, N9), spread arguments (N10), a crash on annotated rest parameters (N2), `as` copies (N3), new PTS1001s on nested arrays' `.par` views, fresh results and `concat` (R6-F4, N4, N5) and `unknown` sources' own-method callbacks (N15); the container flows (R6-F3, N13) are H10b's. Session 6, after review round 5: beyond R5-F2's callbacks given by reference, the parent found writes lost silently, where the base threw, through callbacks given to the narrowed instance's own methods (untyped or annotated), to user methods of uninferred narrowings and of members, through functions in object and array literal arguments, through reduce's accumulator without an initial value (C283), and through any object held inside a member (an element, a property, a method's result, a `?.` chain; C284), with `===` false between two reads of one object. The reproducer's 777-case matrix: 230 OK on the base, 604 with H10a's first commit, 608 after review round 1 and 622 after round 2, none worse and none wrong; 43 cases the base rejected (PTS1001) where a narrowed value flows out, or a program passes another instance's values, now compile and throw a checked TypeError where they run. The LRU corpus program and both reviewers' probes print what Node prints, so `algorithms/lru_cache_map.pts` is unskipped (inventory 269 compatible, 0 known bugs). Review round 5 (NOT READY, "narrow") found destructured and by-reference callbacks (R5-F2), annotated callback false positives (R5-F1) and `.par` element writes on deep copies (R5-F3). Review round 4 (NOT READY) found the guard rejecting `unknown`, `any` and `catch` sources the base ran (R4-F1) and six paths that still copied (R4-F2). Review round 3 (NOT READY) found copies at 14 other sinks (R3-F2) and the dynamic-local cache shared by instances (R3-F1). Review round 2 (NOT READY) found that round 1's dynamic members were copied into `any[]` and object-typed locals, returns and parameters, losing writes silently where the base threw (R2-F1), missed mapped and index-signature types (R2-F2), private methods called dynamically (R2-F3) and conversions to unrelated classes (R2-F4). Review round 1 (NOT READY) found the first commit's cache across instances (R1-F1), unions with `object` (R1-F7), a compiler crash on tuple members (R1-F4), private getters and private writes and methods of instances for any (R1-F5, R1-F2), narrowed code that no longer compiled (R1-F3), private reads where any value goes (R1-F6), unsound inference (R1-F8), and missed references (R1-F9, R1-F10).
- **Alternatives rejected:**
  - Narrowing to the inferred instance in the checker: tsc would then reject programs it accepts (`const n: number = c.get(k)` is a `number | undefined`), and the editor would show other types.
  - Only dynamic values (the reproducer's design B): slower typed reads, and silently wrong without the C273/C274 fixes; kept as the fallback.
  - Making tsc's `C<any, …>` a dynamic value wherever it types a place (the reproducer's design C) in this slice: it is what values that flow out need, but it changes every `C<any>` place of every program, explicit `Box<any>` too, so it is H10b's, with its own tests and review.
- **Known gaps:** H10b (C279): a narrowed value, or a member tsc types C<any, …>, that flows out of the narrowing into a place tsc types `C<any, …>` (`const m2 = x`, `let n = c.next`, a const a closure captures, `[x]`, `ident(x)`, a function's inferred return type, the element `find` or `filter` gives with TS 5.5's inferred predicate, `.par` over those, `c.byKey.get(k)?.v`) compiles and throws a checked TypeError where it runs; private writes and private method calls on an instance held as a dynamic value that isn't an instance for any throw TypeError where JS runs them (C274's rest). An interface whose member absorbs a type parameter may hold an instance the structural inference doesn't give, a checked TypeError, as on the base (R2-F5: a `Cache<string, number>` holding a `MapLRU<string, number | undefined>`, whose `get` returns `V | undefined`). Members of narrowed instances that tsc types with any are read, written and called as dynamic values: a hot loop over `c.items[i]` is several times slower than through a typed parameter (R2-F6; deferred to H27, DEFERRED.md#h10a). The honest PTS1001s where a copy would be kept or changed (`fillAny(c.items)`, `return c.items` with an inferred return type, `c.items ?? []`, a default parameter, `.par.sort()` of a member) also reject such code in branches that never run, which the base compiled (R3-F3, accepted: a silent copy is worse). A value of such a narrowing stored in a variable declared `any` and later converted to a typed place is D-179's copy, as for any `any` value (R4-F2's `s04b`). A function given by reference, or inside an object or array literal argument, to a method of such a value with a parameter that would copy (`c.items.forEach(pushOne)` with `arr: any[]`, `c.run({ cb: (items) => items.push(4) })`) is PTS1001 also where the method never hands that parameter the instance's objects (a user method that hands it a fresh array) or the function, of code not known there (a method, a `let`, a parameter), only reads it; a function in a literal argument would need the literal's shape to take its dynamic parameters (session 6, C283). A function given by reference whose `this` parameter or overloads hide its code is judged as one of unknown code. Objects inside such values that flow into a new container (a spread `[...c.matrix]`, `entries()`, `new Set(c.matrix)`, `Array.from(c.store.values())`, `new Map(c.store)`, `values().next().value`, `Promise.all(c.matrix)`) or a promise's by-reference `.then(f)` are copied into the container's tsc type, so writes through them are lost silently where the base threw: H10b's flows out of the narrowing (C285; skipped `run/generic_instanceof_nested_objects_into_containers.pts`). Objects inside such values stored into typed sinks that keep or change them (an annotated local, `{ row: c.matrix[0] }`, `[c.matrix[0]]`, a field, `kept.push(…)`, `store.set(k, …)`, `yield`, an arrow's body, a return: R6-F4) are PTS1001 also where only read or never reached, as members are (R3-F3's trade-off; `errors/generic_instanceof_nested_object_returned.pts`). A by-reference function of known code that only reads a Set or Map parameter (`s.size`) is PTS1001 (`onlyReads` knows arrays; N7). A member's array whose rows are the instance's (`o.matrix`, also from an uninferred source) given to a helper whose `any[][]` parameter only reads it is PTS1001 where the base, fix5 and fix6 copied it, since the copy's rows are copies (R6-F1's rule; review round 7's `z_deep_read_only_param.pts`). An assignment that stores a function into such a value is judged by tsc's type of the stored value, so a function written there untyped (`c.cfg.onRows = (rows) => …`) is PTS1001 as its parameter takes tsc's `any[][]`. A function read through such a value into a place with a type (`const h: (v: number) => string = s.listeners[0]`, an array literal `[c.cb, c.cb]`, `new Set([c.cb])`) is an adapter there, so `===` with the instance's own is false (the latter two are C285's container flows). The program's function stored where the instance's function type goes (`c.listeners.push(log)` for a `log(v: any)`) is an adapter there, so `indexOf(log)` misses it, as in typed code without instanceof (review round 10's `b03`, `b03b`; C288's class). A function given to `concat`, `with` or `toSpliced` of such a value with a copying parameter is PTS1001 even where the result is only used locally. Checked TypeErrors where Node runs (the base threw at the narrowing in the same programs; review round 12's notes): an object literal pushed into a member array of object types through such a value (`c.groups.push({ list: [], name: "g" })`: `expected {list: number[]; name: string}, got Object`) or returned by a callback to the narrowed instance's typed method (`c.make(() => ({ list: [3] }))`), since a dynamic value's object converts to a shape only by a check its literal's shape fails (C289 → H29); `.then(…)` on a Promise member held as a dynamic value (`c.pending.then is not a function`, C147's class, H29). A function value held as `any` given to such a method, a by-reference function that casts an `object` or `unknown` parameter to `any[]` and writes it, and an `unknown` source holding an instance for any (`Grid<any>`) whose nested array converts to a typed `number[]` by a copy (`===` false where fix5 was PTS1001) lose writes as D-179 and D-071 convert any value (R6-F7, N11; C288 → H29, with `s04b`). In typed code, D-094's copy for a parameter only read or a new array copies nested arrays too, so writes to rows a callee reads out are lost (`firstPush(rows: any[][])` of a `number[][]`, C286 → H22); `a.concat(z)` of an `any` z holding an array throws where Node concatenates (C287 → H29); an evolving `let s;` given such an array and `s.push(x)` is C231's `Array.isArray` copy (H22). Elsewhere: variance through `any` (`Cache<string, number | string>` holding a `MapLRU<string, number>`) is PTS1001 at the upcast, before any narrowing (C280 → H29); a generic interface's getter read through the interface (C275 → H29); messages name instances without type arguments (C277 → H22).
- **Tests:** the reproducer's `run/generic_instanceof_{interface_source,base_class_source,nongeneric_source,union_source,type_arguments,type_parameter_source,two_instances_one_place,forms,own_class_methods,new_own_instance_in_method,private_field_dynamic,dynamic_member_writes}.pts` (RED on the base) and guards `generic_instanceof_guard_{dynamic_sources,same_class,nongeneric,lying_predicate}.pts`; `generic_instanceof_par_callbacks.pts` (threads 1 2 4), `generic_instanceof_union_conflicting_instances.pts`, `generic_instanceof_generic_function_instances.pts`; 27 from review round 1's probes (`generic_instanceof_{class_method_instances,generic_function_any_instance,union_object_member,union_empty_object,tuple_member,tuple_member_forms,private_getter,private_static,any_instance_base,any_instance_private_method,any_instance_object_source,unreached_branch,unreached_const_copy,member_uses_{a,b,c},argument_forms_{a,b,c},private_read_uses,private_in_callback,unique_instances,type_parameter_object,reference_kinds,imported_reference}.pts`, `generic_instanceof_{private_par,dynamic_member_par}.pts` at threads 1 2 4); 17 from review round 2's probes and round 1's member chains (`generic_instanceof_{mapped_members,member_uses_d,dynamic_call_results,member_local_alias,private_name_other_instance,optional_dynamic_calls,member_object_alias,unknown_object_alias,private_method_literal_argument,private_function_field,uninferred_member_alias,unreached_record_member,nested_generic_members,nested_parts,optional_chains,self_referential_members,private_chain}.pts`) and `errors/generic_instanceof_{unrelated_class_target,member_passed_to_mutator,member_returned}.pts`; 9 from review round 3's probes (`generic_instanceof_{local_across_instances,local_across_instances_reversed,local_cache_order,local_cache_order_control,dynamic_local_lifetimes,dynamic_local_suspensions,dynamic_local_par,dynamic_local_builtins,dynamic_local_scopes}.pts`) and `errors/generic_instanceof_{uninferred_value_sinks,dynamic_value_yield_sinks}.pts` and one error test per sink of r15 that is PTS1001 (`errors/generic_instanceof_sink_*.pts`, 9); 6 from review round 4's probes (`generic_instanceof_{unknown_member_reads,catch_member_reads,any_member_reads,callback_and_await_paths,bypass_paths,sinks_that_keep_the_object}.pts`) and `errors/generic_instanceof_shorthand_copy.pts` (round 4's `errors/generic_instanceof_annotated_callback_copy.pts` is session 6's `run/generic_instanceof_annotated_callback_writes.pts`, which runs as Node); skipped for H10b `generic_instanceof_{flows_out,unknown_flows_out,par}.pts`; `generic_instanceof_dynamic_member_locals.pts` (a local bound to a dynamic value's member, which D-179 copied); `lower_test.go`'s `TestInstanceofNarrowsToTheInstanceTheDeclaredTypeImplies`; the corpus's `algorithms/lru_cache_map.pts`.
- **Tests (session 6):** `run/generic_instanceof_{callback_references,callback_own_methods,annotated_callback_writes,nested_objects,nested_objects_dynamic_sources}.pts` (the first, which reproduced R5-F2 skipped, now holds the forms that run: a function expression, by-reference functions whose array parameter is `any` or `unknown` or only read, a reduce accumulator of their own, a rest parameter, a comparator); `errors/generic_instanceof_callback_{reference_function,reference_const_arrow,reference_object_method,reference_parameter,user_method_reference,member_method_reference,map_foreach_reference,nested_object,nested_array,nested_literal,reduce_without_initial_reference}.pts`, one PTS1001 each; after review round 6, `run/generic_instanceof_{casts_and_callbacks,nested_objects_reads,member_chain_compile_time}.pts`, the skipped `run/generic_instanceof_nested_objects_into_containers.pts` (H10b) and `errors/generic_instanceof_{callback_deep_only_reads,callback_hands_itself,callback_kept_handler,callback_reduce_initial_instance,callback_spread_argument,member_deep_read_only,nested_object_returned}.pts`; after review round 7, `run/generic_instanceof_reduce_own_accumulators.pts` and `errors/generic_instanceof_kept_function_{push,element,field,array_literal}.pts`; after review round 8, `run/generic_instanceof_function_identity.pts` and `errors/generic_instanceof_kept_function_{destructuring,object_assign}.pts`; after review round 9, `run/generic_instanceof_own_function_values.pts` and `errors/generic_instanceof_mixed_{nullish_store,conditional_local,conditional_argument,concat_store}.pts`; after review round 10, `run/generic_instanceof_own_function_logical.pts` and `errors/generic_instanceof_kept_function_derived_{concat,with}.pts`; after review round 11, `run/generic_instanceof_own_function_nullish_optionals.pts` and `run/generic_instanceof_own_functions_to_concat.pts`; round 3's `errors/generic_instanceof_sink_as_any.pts` runs as Node now (in `casts_and_callbacks`).
- **Supersedes:** D-149's known gap for `instanceof` narrowing to a generic class from an interface or another class.

### D-219 · tsc's `C<any, …>` is any instance of C, held as a dynamic value; private writes, calls and `#f in o` on dynamic values go to the declaring class's instances; new containers of an instance's objects hold them as they are (H10b)
- **Date:** 2026-10-24
- **Decision:**
  - **`C<any, …>` places (C279; the H10 reproducer's design C)** (`isAnyInstance`, `holdsAnyInstance`, `anyInstanceIn` in `pts/lower/any_instances.go`): for a generic class C of the program (not of the `pts:` library, whose code uses `ParallelArray<any>` as a class), tsc's C<any, …> is the type `instanceof` narrows to where the declared type doesn't name C (D-218), the type of every place such a value flows to (`const m2 = x`, a const a closure captures, `[x]`, `{ m: x }`, `ident(x)`, an inferred return type, the element `find`/`filter` give with TS 5.5's inferred predicate, `.par` over those, a Map's values, `await`, `yield`), and the type a program writes as `Box<any>` or infers from an `any` argument (`new Box(raw)`). In TypeScript such a place holds any of C's instances. `irType` gives it as a dynamic value, so any instance goes in as it is and its members are read, written and called at run time (`isAny`), a write of a value its instance's field doesn't take being D-070's TypeError as through `any`; an intersection whose covering member is C<any> (`S & C<any>`) is one too. Code that needs the class itself gets C's instance for any (`classOf`): `new C<any>()`, a superclass, `this` and `super`. A spread, a rest pattern or a spread into a Record of such a value copies C's fields, read at run time (`spreadNames`), and `call`, `apply` and `bind` of a function held as a dynamic value are found at run time; `o.m.call(self, …)`, `apply` and `bind` for an o held as a dynamic value (a C<any, …> place's, an `any`) run o's method on self where self's class has the same method (`dynamicMethodWithThis`, the runtime's `pts_dynamic_method_for`), else TypeError (R2-F6; a method read as a value stays bound to its object, D-044). A generic method whose result may hold generic class instances (`map<R>(f): Box<R>`), called on such a value (a place, not a narrowing's own value or a narrowed `unknown`/`any`, which stay calls through any), is called as on C's instance for any with a checked downcast of the receiver (`callsAnyInstanceGenericMethod`, `classReceivers`), also in chains and recursion (R3-F2): as the base did for the instance for any, a TypeError for another instance, where the erased copy found through `any` would make an instance for any where tsc says `Box<R>` (R2-F5).
  - **Conversions of such values** (review round 1's R1-F1, R1-F7): they are what H10a's uninferred narrowings are (`isUninferredInstanceof`), so H10a's nested-object, container and dynamic-local rules apply, except to variables declared `unknown` or `any` and their members (D-179, R4-F1). D-218's compile-time guards (a copy that would be changed or kept, a callback by reference or a stored function with a copying parameter) apply only to values an `instanceof` narrowing itself made dynamic (`dynamicSource`'s modes: held, made, narrowed only; `narrowedByInstanceof`). A value of a C<any, …> place without a narrowing that goes into a place of a type that holds dynamic values (`any[]`, an annotated local, a return, a parameter, `??`, a destructuring assignment, a rest object's or spread's property, an interface or shape holding one) is taken as it is (`asIsConversion`, `convertMember`, `ir.FromUnion.AsIs`, the runtime's `pts_value_convert_as_is`): C's instance for any's `any[]` itself, so writes reach it, and for another instance (a Box<number>'s `number[]`) `TypeError: Expected value[], got Array, which converts to it only as a copy that wouldn't show later changes`, never a silent copy. A new container of such values (`[b.items]`, `b.matrix.map(f)`, `new Set(b.matrix)`, `Object.entries(b.rec)`, the array a rest parameter gets from a spread) may itself be copied, what it holds only as it is (`ir.FromUnion.Fresh`, `pts_value_convert_fresh`; R2-F1). A function of the program given by reference to such a value's method (also `.then`), or stored in it, whose parameter would get a copy, goes in as it is where that value is C's instance for any, tested at run time (`anyInstanceFunction`, `anyInstanceRoot`), so its identity holds (`off(f)` finds it, R3-F1); for another instance it is wrapped in an adapter that takes its arguments as they are (`asIsAdapter`; R2-F2), a TypeError where the instance's code would hand it copies; one the adapter can't wrap (defaults, a rest parameter) goes as through any (R3-F5). An array literal of such values with a typed array context (`const c: any[][] = [b.items]`) is a literal of that type, its elements taken as they are (R3-F3); spreads of such values' object-typed members copy tsc's properties at run time, and of hash-map-typed members into a Record their entries (R3-F4). Into typed places (`number[]`, `Map<string, number>`) and into fields it converts as through `any` (D-071's documented checked copies; R1-F7).
  - **Destructuring** (R1-F6, R2-F4): a name an object pattern binds from a value tsc types C<any, …>, or a pattern nested in one binds (`for (const { items } of boxes)`, `({ meta: { tags } }: Box<any>)`, callback patterns: `destructuresAnyInstance`, by the patterns' types), is a dynamic local, as `const { items } = b` is.
  - **Private members of dynamic values (C274's rest)** (`familyPrivateMember`, `familyPrivatePlace`, `familyPrivateCall`, `privateDeclaration`; `ir.FamilySetField`, `ir.FamilyCall`; cgen's `familySetField`, `familyCall`, `collectFamilyMethods`): `o.#f = v`, compound updates (`+=`, `++`, read once), `o.#m(…)` and calls of a private field's function, for any o held as a dynamic value (narrowed by instanceof, in a C<any, …> place, declared `any`), act on whichever of the declaring class's instances (or the one non-generic class) o is: a written value converted to the field's type with a check, a method's arguments by its dynamic entry (a generic private method by each instance's erased copy, D-078), o's class found before the arguments are evaluated (JS reads `o.#m` first); another object gets V8's TypeError (`Cannot write private member #f to an object whose class did not declare it`, `Receiver must be an instance of class C`; reads, D-218: `Cannot read private member #f …`). A private field holding an array or map stays the instance's (R1-F8). The private member is found lexically, since tsc gives no symbol for one on an `any`. Private methods called so get dynamic entries but stay out of method tables, where `o["#m"]` would find them. `#f in o` tests every instance of the declaring generic class, which are one class in JS (`privateIn`; the base tested only the instance being lowered, wrong for another instance, and was PTS9001 in a static method), for dynamic receivers too, whose primitive throws V8's `Cannot use 'in' operator to search for '#v' in 5`; a static private's brand is the class's own, false for any value here, since classes aren't values (R2-F7). Containers of functions of such an instance (a private array of callbacks) stay its own too (R2-F8). H10a's exception that kept the downcast to C<any, …> for private writes and calls (`isPrivateMemberChange`) is gone.
  - **New containers of an instance's objects (C285)** (`isInstanceofContainer`, `dynamicCollectionOf`, `isInstanceofIteratorStep`, `holdsInstanceofContainer` in `pts/lower/instance_containers.go`): an array literal with such elements or spreads, `Array.of(…)`, `Array.from(x)`, `Object.values(x)`, `Object.entries(x)`, `new Map(x)` and `new Set(x)` whose tsc element (key, value) type would copy an object that an instanceof narrowing holds as a dynamic value (`copiesInstanceObjects`), and an iterator's step (`next()`, `return(v)`, `throw(e)`) whose value would, hold dynamic values (an `any[]`, a map or set of dynamic keys or values, an iterator result of a dynamic value) and are themselves held as dynamic values, also through a local bound to one, so their elements are found at run time and stay the instance's objects; `for…of` over such an iterator binds dynamic items; `Promise.all`, `allSettled`, `any` and `race` of such a value are seen through as `Promise.resolve` was (R4-F2), and an awaited one stays dynamic for every source, `unknown` and `any` too. A function given by reference to `.then`, `.catch` or `.finally` of such a promise with a copying parameter, and such rows spread into a copying rest parameter (`pushRest(...c.matrix)`), are D-218's PTS1001; containers given to a parameter that only reads them, or under a read-only `.par` view, take D-218's R6-F1/R5-F3 rule, as `c.matrix` itself does (R1-F10). `copiesDynamicValue` asks `irType` of a contextual type quietly, since a library function's parameter (`Array.from`'s `ArrayLike<T>`) may be a type no value is converted to.
  - **Declared types of references** (review round 1's note `p13`, an H10a bug): `declaredTypeOfReference` instantiates a property's or element's type, so a generic class's own code in its instance for any (`this.next.items` of a `ListNode<T> | null`) isn't taken for an instanceof narrowing.
- **Reason:** C279, C285 and C274's rest (H10b, D-218's Known gaps). tsc gives an explicit `Box<any>` and a narrowing's flows the same type, so places can't be told apart by type; treating C<any, …> as "any instance" is TypeScript's meaning, covers every flow at once, and turns the base's TypeErrors and PTS1001s into Node's behaviour (tie-breakers 2 and 3). The checked downcast to C<any, …> (D-149, D-218's `classCast`) could only succeed for an instance for any, which a narrowing's object rarely is. Review round 1 (NOT READY, R1-F1–F14) showed that applying D-218's compile-time guards to every such place broke programs that only use `Box<any>` and ran on the base; the as-is conversion keeps those working, as the base did, and turns a would-be silent copy for other instances into a TypeError (tie-breaker 3: a run-time check over a compile error).
- **Alternatives rejected:**
  - A demand by name for generic methods called on C<any, …> values (as through unions of object types, D-191): it made the compiler run away on `pair<U>(u: U): Box<[T, U]>` (polymorphic recursion through the demanded instances).
  - PTS1001 for C<any, …> places' callbacks and stores with copying parameters (snapshot 1): `Box<any>`-only programs the base ran would stop compiling; the adapter keeps them running.
  - Typing every flow by inference (the H10 reproducer's "A must type the member expressions derived from the reference too"): each generic call, inferred return and predicate would need its own inference, and explicit `Box<any>` places would stay PTS1001.
  - Only places made from narrowings: tsc gives both the same type object.
  - D-218's PTS1001 for every C<any, …> place's copies (snapshot 1): it rejects `Box<any>`-only programs the base ran (R1-F1).
  - D-071's copy for them, as through `any`: silent for another instance's arrays.
  - Keeping places typed where the type argument any came from substituting a type parameter (`ListNode<T>` in the instance for any), for speed: such a field then refuses other instances that TypeScript lets in, and it is a semantic shortcut for an optimisation (group D); deferred.
  - PTS1001 where a new container would copy an instance's objects (R6-F4's rule for typed sinks): Node's behaviour can be had.
- **Costs:** a C<any, …> place reads and writes at run time: 1M iterations of a write, a read and a method call through `const hot = new Box<any>(0)` run 140.8M instructions on the base and 761.8M (callgrind, 5.4×); review round 1 measured 2.7–6.5× for locals, parameters, arrays and a stack, and about 15× for a generic list's own traversal in its instance for any (R1-F5); deferred to H27 (DEFERRED.md#h10b). Fewer instance-for-any classes are made where a program only names `C<any, …>` as a type.
- **Known gaps:** a function given by reference to `.then` of a promise of an `unknown` or `any` source's member, with a copying parameter, gets a copy (R1-F13; C288's class); a C<any, …> place holding another instance converts into a typed place or a field by D-071's copy (R1-F7, documented); `Box<Box<any>>` places and `Box<any> & {…}` intersections are PTS1001 (H29); a Box<number> held in a C<any, …> place doesn't convert to an interface whose member is `any` (C280's variance, H29); `o["#g"]` through `any` finds a private getter by name, and a static private read through `any` gives undefined (H29); `#f in o` of a primitive held as a dynamic value is false where JS throws; typed code's promise paths copy a `number[]` into an `any[]` callback parameter silently (the reproducer's root cause 10, H22); `new Set([1, "1", true])` as a `Set<any>` is PTS1001 (H29).
- **Tests:** see the H10b evidence and SPEC §9.144.
- **Supersedes:** D-218's checked conversion where tsc's C<any, …> goes (`classCast`'s H10a case) and its `isPrivateMemberChange` exception; D-218's Known gaps C279, C285 and C274's rest.
